# quest-map.md — Cross-Source Quest Map


Very important. Use the API by running queries on curl to localhost 1367 by using node.js to start wbapi-server.js we can the query exiting cities,  and quests. Mission bits. If you can't get a request for your type, consider asking the user to add to plan.md 

*The thread that ties all book quests together. Read this before writing any new quest.*

> **Purpose:** Track the theme, location, and quest identity of every book we
> process. When writing new quests, read this file first — find what is already
> in the weave, avoid repeating themes, look for complementary angles.
> Cross-reference with `../story.md` for the game's actual city contexts and
> node aesthetics before placing a quest act in a city.

> **How it grows:** After writing each book's Quest API Stub, append one line:
> `{CODE} | {quest title} | Cycle N | {node/city} | {core theme in 5 words}`

---

## The Overarching Story We Are Building

These quests are not separate events. They are scenes in a single long story about
a Fighter who moves through the world carrying things — objects, words, obligations
— that belong to other people. The Fighter is not the hero of history. History
moves around them. They hold the token. They deliver it. They witness.

Each source book gives us one scene. Each scene adds one more kind of loyalty
tested, one more kind of grief carried, one more kind of courage proven in the
dark. Across all the books, the Fighter accumulates a life: the horn they kept
full, the shield they buried, the cloak that was torn, the garland that burned.

The game asks: what does it mean to keep your word when the world does not?

| JRS | Armida's Conversion | 6 | DAM → CON → WM (Damascus → Constantinople → Weimar) | Conversion Records — The Former Enemy's Declaration Made Under Her Own Authority |
| JRS | The Godfrey Dispatch | 7 | PKR → RME → WM (Pilgrim's Quarter → Rome → Weimar) | Commander's Dispatches — The Account That Names Its Own Differences From the Competing Version |
| ATH | The Herald's Tablet | 1 | IDC → SKN → WM (refugee camp → Scaean Gate ruins → Weimar archive) | The last just act of a city before its burning must be preserved separately from the burning |
| ATH | Helen at the Funeral | 2 | TRH → WM (Troy harbor post-sack → archive) | The account from the cause of the war; the voice that crosses the line of allegiance because grief recognizes itself; filed under her name in the primary records |
| ATH | The Embassy's Offer | 3 | IDC → CON → WM | The unsigned offer that acknowledged the debt without discharging it; both the honor-debt and the refusal in the same record; archive: Rejected Offers Under Divine Compulsion — Records Where the Ordinary Terms of Negotiation Did Not Apply, first entry |
| ATH | Patroclus's Commission | 4 | IDC → RGS → WM | The complete commission record — the tactical limit and the personal stake named simultaneously; the archive files unanswerable things; archive: Commission Records — Instructions Given Before the Fatal Decision, first entry |
| ATH | Andromache's Loom | 5 | TRH → VS → WM | The interrupted domestic record as military record; the war appears at the weft-break where the shuttle was dropped; archive: Interrupted Work Records — The Unfinished Thing That Shows the Shape of What the Interruption Was, first entry |
| ATH | The Shield's Description | 6 | SKN → CON → WM | The world engraved as armor the night before it was used; the god who made both things and understood what he was doing; archive: Divine Craft Records — Objects Described Before Their Destruction, first entry |
| ATH | Hecuba's Supplication | 7 | SKN → RME → WM | The correctly executed petition that was denied; the gods' decision in the record alongside the human act; archive: Supplication Records — Correctly Executed Petitions That Were Denied, first entry |
| ZTH | The Wax Account | 1 | SIT → PRT → WM (scribe's port → port registry → Weimar archive) | The bound man's incomplete account is the only survivable form of overwhelming knowledge; the gaps are the data |
| ZTH | Iphitus's Provenance | 2 | MSN → ITH (Messene guest-gift witness → Ithaca estate registry) | The guest-gift carries obligations that survive the object's use; the bow was never household property; the suitors' contest was invalid from the start |
| ZTH | The Unraveling Chronicle | 3 | ITH → CON → WM | The physical evidence of patience is usually consumed in the act of patience; this cloth survived because the ruse was completed; the worn thread is not a document of failure but of capacity; archive: Patience Records — Three Years of Nightly Unraveling, first entry |
| ZTH | Eumaeus's Twenty Years | 4 | ITH → RGS → WM | The informal accounts kept without commission or legal authority; twenty years of loyalty under occupation visible only in the format of the document; archive: Faithful Household Accounts — Twenty Years of Record Under Occupation, first entry |
| ZTH | Nausicaa's Letter | 5 | PHC → RME → WM | The letter written for the writer rather than the reader; the sealed document that authenticates its own unread condition; archive: Unsent Letters — Records Written for the Writer Rather Than the Reader, first entry |
| ZTH | Anticlea's Message | 6 | SIT → CON → WM | The testimony delivered by the dead; the message complete, true, and received but unconfirmable; archive: Underworld Testimony Records — Messages Delivered by the Dead, first entry |
| ZTH | The Cattle Account | 7 | SIT → RME → WM | The sole survivor's reconstruction of the decision that made him the sole survivor; filed alongside the divine complaint as together the most complete account available; archive: Reconstructed Decisions — Accounts of Acts the Sole Survivor Was Not Witness To, first entry |
| FCO (II) | Sunt Lacrimae Rerum | 1 | NSM → APT → WM (Naples Scriptorium → Alpine Transit Gate → Weimar archive) | The enemy made your monument; the record of grief is preserved by those who caused it; without them you would not be in the archive |
| FCO (II) | Palinurus's Shore | 2 | SVA → PAC → NMR (Sibyl's vault archive Cumae → Cape Palinurus harbor town → Naples maritime registry) | The naming that was sworn before the death — the Sibyl's prophecy as founding geographic record predating any administrative authority; the only inheritance of the navigator who died to guarantee the passage |
| NWI-01 | What the Sea Knows | 1 | CPL → BLK → TBZ → WM | An army without a patron becomes its own republic; what holds it together is the vote — the willingness of strangers to choose each other when every hierarchy has failed; Unilateral Statements — the decision that reconstitutes an army |
| WAW-01 | The Arbiter's Farewell | 1 | SAU → VFM → BLO → WM | When a man cannot prevent the burning and will not participate in it, the only act remaining is the letter; the final document names what happened in terms the perpetrator cannot control; Unilateral Statements — Acts of Final Clarity |
| WAW-02b | The Arena Register | 2 | NAP → BLO → WM | The administrative record that captured what it could not name; the official document trusted because the recorder had no stake in the outcome and no category for what he saw; Administrative Records of Uninterpreted Events |
| NWI-02b | The Towns That Were Not Given (I) | 2 | BTR → CON → RGS → WM | The promise kept by one side and broken by the other; the grant record filed under the army, not the towns, because the army was the party that kept its word |
| MLA-01 | The Third Comparison | 1 | LMO → THA → ANC → WM | The comparison produces what neither biography contains alone; meaning lives in the gap between two lives, not in either separately |
| MLA-02b | The Missing Preface | 2 | NXS → RGS → WM | The preface that outlived its Lives — the method statement in its purest form, unqualified by the illustrations it was written to introduce; the archive creates a new category: primary methodological document, argument standing independent of its evidence |
| BGW | The Night's Account | 1 | CAI → FAM → WM (Cairo booksellers' quarter → Famagusta factor's house → Weimar archive) | Every story she told was one night of survival; the collection is what she made with the time she bought; the archive receives the life, not the instrument |
| CAI | The Seventh Day | 1 | ALB → ANT → WM (Mar Marun Monastery → Antioch factor's house → Weimar archive) | The story told to delay an execution changes the shape of the listener; the seven days are evidence accumulated, not time bought |
| BEY | The Sober Draft | 1 | LGE → ARD → WM (Saint-Laurent Monastery → Ardennes road junction → Weimar archive) | The draft that stops at the evidence limit is truer than the finished book; the compiler is also a witness — to what he refused to invent |
| HTY-01 | Savitri's Three Bargains | 1 | TBZ → TRB → WM | The bargains were logically irrefutable; she gave Death nothing he did not already own and took back everything she came for; the archive creates a category for the argument that actually worked on Death |
| HTY-02 | Draupadi's Plaint | 2 | NIS → TBZ → WM | The question refused without being answered survives as legal form; the archive is the first institution to give it a filing date |
| HTY-03 | Bhishma's Record | 3 | TBZ → SAM → WM | The physician's vocabulary for what the warrior called a spiritual act; the clinical record and the theological claim are the same sentence; the archive receives both |
| HTY-04 | The Standards Folio | 4 | TBZ → BRS → WM | The painted record before the text tradition; the illuminator is the primary source; no Latin or Arabic text describes these warrior standards with colors and positions |
| HTY-05 | The Dice Analysis | 5 | NIS → TBZ → WM | The technical analysis of divine unfairness; the gambling manual as a theological argument the gambler did not mean to write; the archive gives the question a filing date |
| HTY-06 | The Sami Tree | 6 | GNJ → TBZ → WM | Military concealment as ritual disguise; the tactic inseparable from the theology; the archive creates a new category for the document that is both simultaneously |
| HTY-07 | The Final Form | 7 | NIS → BK → WM | The transformation that did not hold; the iron cursed to grass reemerged as iron and fulfilled its purpose; no transformation removes what an object was made for, only defers it |
| ADA | The Curlew's Grief | 1 | SUL → TBZ (Sultaniya scholar's house → Tabriz poet's court recitation) | The shape of grief becomes the shape of art; the first poem in the world was born from witnessed suffering, and the word for verse and the word for grief share a root |
| ADA | Sita Into the Earth | 2 | BAG → BAS → WM (Baghdad Sufi scholar's study → Basra scholar's household → Weimar) | Justice and vindication are not the same thing; the earth was her choice of court; the archive creates a new category for the distinction |
| ADA | The Bow No God Could String | 3 | ERZ → TRB → WM | The bow's specifications are the proof that only a god could string it; the technical refutation becomes a theological argument; archive files under specifications that cannot be replicated without the legend's conditions |
| ADA | Kaikeyi's Ledger | 4 | BAG → MOS → WM | The deferred favor is the weapon with no visible edge; the king could not refuse it because he had given his word before he had the authority to deny; archive files under instruments of succession |
| ADA | The Sandals on the Throne | 5 | TBZ → MRG → WM | The fourteen years of unsent letters are the evidence that faithful waiting is a form of speech; the archive files it under regent's records and adds a note: speech to someone who could not hear it |
| ADA | Hanuman's Report | 6 | ALP → DAM → WM | Military intelligence encoded as merchant route notes; the surveyor crossed 200 leagues of ocean; archive files it as historical military geography of a legendary citadel |
| ADA | The Fire Was Not the Proof | 7 | NIS → MRV → WM | The fire ordeal was legally sufficient; the Fire God testified; the exile violated the proof's terms; archive files under legal theology — proofs that were not honored |
| ADA | Ahalyá's Statement | 8 | BUR → CON → WM | The curse was for recognizing the deceiver and yielding anyway; the law has no category for consent under recognized impersonation; archive cross-files under women's arguments and legal philosophy — category failures |
| ADA | Trishanku's Commission | 9 | CAF → TAN → WM | Trishanku bribed his way into a heaven that didn't exist; a Genoese merchant wants to name the sky region after himself; the astronomer puts both claims in the same annotation box; archive receives contested provenance |
| ADA | The Price of a Son | 10 | TRB → ERZ → WM | The transaction was sale, gift, adoption, and contract simultaneously and therefore none of them; the categories are insufficient; archive files under legal insufficiency records |
| ADA | The Brahmin's Credentials | 11 | SAM → BUK → WM | A thousand years of penance earned the rank; the rank only counted when Vasishtha said the words; the certificate was lost for thirty generations; the lineage scholar read it once and gave it to the archive |
| ADA | The Coats of Bark | 12 | TAN → SAR → WM | The ceremony was designed to be irrevocable and public; the court noble's deposition is the proof it was witnessed; Bharat releases it to the archive from exile |
| ADA | The False Track | 13 | ALE → CAI → WM | The false track was a mercy to the crowd, not a betrayal of the master; what the loyal servant owes the master is not the same as what he owes those who love the master; archive files under divided obligation records |
| ADA | Sita's Vow on the Ganga | 14 | ANT → BYR → WM | The conditional vow made from exile enumerates everything specifically because hope is specific; archive files under conditional commitments made from exile |
| ADA | The Curse of the Blind Parents | 15 | AMY → SIV → WM | The curse was also a prophecy; the parents described rather than caused the form of his death; archive files under deathbed statements that described rather than caused |
| ADA | The Sage's Feast | 16 | CON → THE → WM | Supply so complete the army refused to march is the logistics paradox; the canonical example; archive files under Military Logistics — The Paradox of Abundance |
| ADA | The Crow's Eye | 17 | BUR → NIC → WM | The punisher's obligation shifts at the release point; the crow negotiated the minimum sacrifice rather than submitting; archive files the first case of punishment-as-contract |
| ADA | Javali's Retraction | 18 | DAM → JER → WM | The argument was sincere even after the speaker officially denied it; the scroll ends before the retraction is complete; archive files under testimony whose sincerity survives its own denial |
| ADA | The Saint Who Waited to Die | 19 | SUL → AMD → WM | The penance was a countdown calibrated to the arrival; the saint did not die waiting but died arriving; archive files under acts of completion — penance as precision timing |
| ADA | The Sword That Corrupts by Proximity | 20 | TUN → FEZ → WM | The weapon that confers invincibility cannot be contested by the standard instrument; burial is not avoidance but the legally appropriate categorical response; archive files the first case of categorical disarmament |
| ADA | The Demon in the Stomach | 21 | SIN → ANK → WM | Digestion as sovereign act; Agastya collapsed ingestion and digestion into one intentional act; two scholars arrived at the same principle from opposite traditions; archive files Medical Philosophy — Sovereignty of the Body, first entry |
| ADA | The Golden Deer | 22 | SUL → HAM → WM | The observation was valid at the moment of observation; the demon's deceptive intent does not retroactively invalidate the naturalist's record; archive files Naturalist Records — Single-Observation Phenomena, first entry |
| ADA | Surpanakha's Lecture | 23 | CAI → JER → WM | The intelligence failure analysis was accurate regardless of the speaker's motivation; archive separates argument from speaker; second entry in Documents Where Analytical Quality Exceeds the Speaker's Credibility |
| ADA | Maricha's Confession | 24 | ERZ → TIF → WM | Permanent alteration by superior force as a fourth philosophical category distinct from death, defeat, and transformation; the altered entity retains continuous memory but cannot return; archive files the first entry |
| ADA | What the River Saw | 25 | THE → ADR → WM | The witness without power to intervene still witnessed; inability to act is a credential for impartiality, not a disqualification; archive files Witness Theology — the disinterested witness as most credible |
| ADA | The Vulture's Funeral | 26 | AMY → TRB → WM | The act was voluntary, knowing, and against interest; the honor-obligation follows from the choice regardless of species; the rites were recognition of a debt, not generosity |
| ADA | The Cursed Form | 27 | BAG → BAS → WM | Acts under curse compulsion with full retained memory require a third moral category — awareness without capacity to refuse; archive files the missing category and notes the first act after restoration was correct |
| ADA | The Envoy's Grammar | 28 | BYR → ANT → WM | The diplomatic register creates legal protection; violation proves recognition of the obligation; twelve parallel cases across five traditions confirm the pattern; archive files the first collection of diplomatic protection cases |
| ADA | The Ornaments | 29 | ANT → ALP → WM | Property placed deliberately as evidence of an ongoing crime creates a custodial obligation, not a property right; the placed object is a deposition without a speaker; the victim's claim does not expire while the victim survives |
| ADA | Tara's Warning | 30 | CON → ADR → WM | Accurate counsel is dismissed not because it is wrong but because acting on it would require the authority to change its self-understanding; the Ottoman scholar names the mechanism: the decision not to hear |
| ADA | The Hidden Shot | 31 | ALP → DAM → WM | Vali challenged Rama on warrior ethics; Rama answered in alliance law; the standing question is answered and the method question is silent; the archive files both the answer and the silence |
| ADA | Sampati's Wings | 32 | ERZ → TRB → WM | The testimony that is given completely and received discharges the cost the testifier paid to reach the moment of telling; seven hagiographic parallels confirm the principle cross-traditionally; archive files Physical Confirmation of Truth, first entry |
| ADA | The Enchanted Cave | 33 | AMD → BAS → WM | A space whose existence is attested but whose architecture violates all known principles; two competing frameworks — impossible space vs. magical construction — both preserved without ruling; archive files Impossible Spaces, first entry |
| ADA | Trijata's Dream | 34 | CAI → JER → WM | Prophetic dream reported against the predictor's interest is the highest evidential class; Trijata predicted her own side's destruction; third entry in Documents Where Analytical Quality Exceeds Speaker's Credibility |
| ADA | Sita Refuses the Rescue | 35 | BYR → ANT → WM | The form of a right act has intrinsic meaning; a rescue without the correct form is not the same act; the crest jewel was the token that created the war as the only correct form of return; archive files Intrinsic Procedural Value, first entry |
| ADA | Mandodari Mistaken | 36 | ANT → ALP → WM | Accurate warning preserved in the loser's own archive and released by the successor; the warning plus the defeat proves the outcome was foreseeable; third instance of the Decision Not to Hear (cross-file ADA-30); archive files Documents from Conquered Archives, first entry |
| ADA | Sarama's Truth | 37 | ALE → CAI → WM | Compassion-motivated intelligence is bounded only by the informant's witness; Sarama defected with no material interest; five-category intelligence taxonomy; archive files Intelligence Studies — Informant Types, first entry |
| ADA | The Suppliant's Shelter | 38 | BUR → NIC → WM | The asylum obligation is triggered by approach and vulnerability; suspicion of motive cannot override it; conditional shelter is not shelter; two traditions agree; archive files Asylum Obligation, first entry |
| ADA | Nala's Bridge | 39 | CON → THE → WM | Bridge specifications are extractable and calculable; the timeline is the specific location of the impossible; the archive files the causeway analysis as Impossible Spaces, second entry, with the gap precisely identified |
| ADA | The Serpent Noose | 40 | TRB → SIV → WM | The nāgāstra was a living-creature weapon with mechanical incapacitation and a natural-enemy counter-force built into its design; biological weapons retain their creatures' natural vulnerabilities; archive files Biological Weapon Analysis, first entry |
| ADA | The Magnanimous Dismissal | 41 | BAG → MOS → WM | The warrior code insists victory has conditions; dismissing an opponent without weapons is not mercy or strategy but preserving what victory means; archive files Required Restraint, first entry |
| ADA | Kumbhakarna's Kill | 42 | MOS → ERZ → WM | Methodical passionless killing under conscientious objection raises four unresolved questions; the archive holds them all; cross-file: Moral Status Under Compulsion (ADA-27) |
| ADA | The False Sita | 43 | ERZ → TBZ → WM | A simulated atrocity achieving identical psychological effects raises the question of whether physical harm is required; both positions preserved without ruling; archive files Psychological Warfare — Simulated Atrocity, first entry |
| ADA | Sita Enters the Fire | 44 | TBZ → SUL → WM | Demanding public proof of privately-known innocence communicates that public opinion has authority over private knowledge; the proof succeeds but the demand cannot be unsent; archive files Ethics — Public Proof, first entry |
| ADA | Ravana's Genealogy | 45 | SUL → TBZ → WM | The complete record of a villain is the archive's obligation; the obligation to completeness is the refusal to omit deliberately, not a claim to include all; incomplete-record dissent preserved alongside; archive files Archive Methodology — Obligation to Completeness, first entry |
| ADA | The Suppliant Dove | 46 | BAS → BAG → WM | The asylum obligation's logic goes to life-for-life when the alternative is the sheltered party's death; the cost goes to the self; legal completion and cosmic transcendence readings both preserved; archive files Asylum Obligation, second entry (with ADA-38: complete statement) |
| ADA | The Aditya Hridayam | 47 | BAG → TBZ → WM | The 108-name hymn is a complete cosmological taxonomy embedded in prayer form for transmission; the content is tradition-independent; the form is also transformative; archive files Celestial Taxonomy — The Sun's Functional Taxonomy, first entry; the prayer is the archive's oldest ally |
| ADA | The Wrong Ally | 48 | TBZ → SUL → WM | The alliance obligation is to the cause; when the party's means contradict the cause, the obligations to party and cause diverge; fulfillment reading and betrayal reading both preserved without ruling; archive files Alliance Law — Ends and Means, first entry; ADA cycle complete |
| AMS | The Scarlet Fence Poem | 1 | NIS → TBZ (Nishapur madrasa → Tabriz scholar quarter) | The gift without a sender's name removes the sender from the obligation they've created; the anonymous commission asks nothing in return — the receiver must act from their own judgment alone |
| AMS | The Spirit's Account | 2 | TBZ → BAG → WM (Tabriz physician's study → Baghdad Sufi scholar → Weimar) | The jealousy that continues past death proves the relationship was real; the spirit speaks without social constraint for the first time; the archive files it under Involuntary Testimony |
| AMS-03 | The Cicada's Husk | 3 | TBZ → TRB → WM | The thing left behind when someone flees is not left accidentally; the scarf and the note are one document; the archive files them together as Personal Documents — Objects as Envelopes |
| AMS-04 | The Rainy Night Register | 4 | TRB → CON → WM | Handwriting as testimony; what a hand produces under no observation is the truest record; the archive holds complete arguments courts have not caught up with yet |
| AMS-05 | The Closed File | 5 | TBZ → RGS → WM | The impossible obligation must be delivered somewhere; filing is the only acknowledgment both parties can afford; the tube remains permanently sealed |
| AMS-06 | The Teaching Fan | 6 | SAM → MRV → WM | The gap between what was taught and what was meant; the annotated score is evidence of the asymmetry the teacher could see and the student could not |
| AMS-07 | The Exchanged Fans | 7 | TBZ → MRG → WM | The cipher that proves ownership also disqualifies it; the fan proves and forecloses simultaneously; the exchange already happened |
| KYA | The Derafsh Kaviani Fragment | 1 | TBZ → TRB → CPL → WM (Tabriz Genoese factor's house → Trebizond harbor → Constantinople → Weimar) | The emblem of legitimate rule was a craftsman's work-apron, not a crown; Kavah gave the kings their banner, not the other way around; the archive preserves the only sovereignty that cannot be inherited — only earned |
| KYA | The Two-Falls Custom | 2 | NIS → TBZ → WM (Nishapur legal scholar's study → Tabriz Jalayirid scholar quarter → Weimar) | The legal custom that may have been invented by the man who needed it; the archive cannot rule and creates a new category — Legal Customs of Uncertain Origin — because the question is correctly preserved even when the answer is not |
| HAV | The Articles | 1 | CHI → RHD (Chios notary's office → Rhodes commandery session hall) | The outlaw society that governs itself with more precise rules than the courts that prosecute it; the pirate articles list every wound's value and create obligations honored more faithfully than most official agreements; the distinction between corsair and pirate is a single document's presence or absence, not a moral category |
| HAV | The Admiral's Letter | 2 | FAM → RHD → WM (Famagusta Genoese notary's office → Rhodes customs house → Weimar archive) | The formal exchange of demands between two parties who recognize each other's authority even while denying each other's right — the letter of ultimatum is also a form of recognition; the corsair who writes in the language of treaty claims a status the letter itself creates; the archive creates a new category: Correspondence Between Parties Who Deny Each Other's Right to Exist |
| HAV | The Sunk Boat | 3 | CAF → BK → WM (Caffa Genoese factor → Bruges → Weimar) | Irreversible Commission Records — the commission usage attestation; the boat was sunk before the boarding; the document records the same principle |
| HAV | The Surgeon's Log | 4 | RHD → VEN → WM (Rhodes → Venice → Weimar) | Wound Register Records — the healer holds the most complete record; the cost tabulated against the glory; all the real arithmetic is in it |
| HAV | The Dead Man's Bluff | 5 | FAM → CON → WM (Famagusta → Constantinople → Weimar) | Nullification Records — the dissolution stamp cannot reach back before it existed; the commission declared dead and never was |
| HAV | The Letter of Marque | 6 | CHI → RME → WM (Chios Genoese commune → Rome → Weimar) | Commission Records — the single document that is the only difference between a crime and a license |
| HAV | The Eyewitness Account | 7 | RHD → LDN → WM (Rhodes → London → Weimar) | Non-Interested Witness Records — the account written by the man nobody tried to impress; the testimony that cannot be dismissed as advocacy |
| CLJ-01 | The Compiled Account | 1 | BIS → KLZ → WM | The proof exists in the compilation — each witness separately is deniable; assembled by someone who understood that independent agreement is the only argument that cannot be accused of collusion, they become evidence; Compiled Witness Records — Independent Accounts That Cannot Be Accused of Collaboration |
| CLJ-02 | The Last Letter | 2 | KLZ → SIB → WM | The letter written in the interval before possible transformation — truth-telling sharpest when there is nothing left to protect; the archive creates a new category: Documents of the Final Interval — written in the knowledge of possible self-loss |
| CLJ-03 | The Property Deed | 3 | KLZ → BDA → WM | The legal instrument performs exactly what it was designed to perform; technically correct throughout; instrument of catastrophe with an unrecognized purpose clause; Legal Documents — Technically Correct Instruments With Unrecognized Purpose Clauses |
| CLJ-04 | The Three Letters in One Day | 4 | SIB → BIS → WM | The first acceptance makes the others false; but they exist in the record permanently; the selection does not unmake the offering; Correspondence Records — Simultaneous Obligations of Unequal Outcome |
| CLJ-05 | The Phonograph Record | 5 | SIB → KLZ → WM | The hesitation mark is as close as anyone can get to the voice; transcription loses the evidence of conviction; Spoken Record Proxies — Transcription Documents Including Real-Time Notation of Delivery Quality |
| CLJ-06 | The Box of Earth | 6 | BOR → BIS → WM | The thing that sustains you is the same thing that limits you; consecration transforms power source into witness object; Transformation Records — Objects That Were Power Sources and Are Now Evidence of Their Neutralization |
| CLJ-07 | The Haarlem Garlic | 7 | SIB → BOR → WM | The protective measure that looks mundane; inspector's skepticism and physician's urgency are both rational; Medical Delivery Records — Urgent Protective Preparations With Non-Standard Methods |
| CLJ-08 | Van Helsing's Contingency | 8 | KLZ → WM | Success is silence; failure is the archive; contingency letter makes irreplaceable knowledge transferable; Contingency Records — Expert Knowledge Transferred in Anticipation of the Expert's Unavailability |
| CLJ-09 | The Transcript | 9 | BIS → WM | Transcription is translation from private knowledge to public record; making legible is the final act; Transcription Records — Private Knowledge Made Public: Certification by Factual Witness; entry 15 connects to Ágoston property from CLJ-03 |
| BLQ | The Saint's Attestation | 1 | FLR → NAP (Florence guild counting house → Naples ecclesiastical court filing desk) | The document is formally correct in every claim it makes and substantively fraudulent in every conclusion it supports; the worst man alive is certified as a saint by a record that is technically accurate; the carrier never opens the seal |
| BLQ | The Court Defense | 2 | PIS → PRA (Pistoia retired judge's house → Prato podestà's court clerk) | The law that protects already exists and only needs to arrive in time; the document renders the opposing argument moot by existing; the carrier who brings truth has no advantage over the carrier who brings fraud unless speed is the only variable |
| BLQ-03 | The Three Rings | 3 | GEN → RGS → CON | The document whose value is its unread state; three parties hold incompatible beliefs about its contents; the carrier's task is to preserve the ambiguity continuously through multiple encounters; Diplomatic Records — Ambiguous Instruments Sustaining Multiple Incompatible Expectations Until Official Opening |
| BLQ-04 | The Coals | 4 | FLR → Fiesole → Monte di Croce → BOL | Authentication through the carrier's performance, not the object's truth; the box is opened mid-journey and the original contents stolen; recover the relic or deliver the honest coal; Evidence of Attempted Theft — Objects Submitted as Formal Record of a Crime Against a Commission |
| BLQ-05 | The Falcon's Inventory | 5 | FLR → Ferrara → Mestre → VEN | The document that outlives what it records; deed of transfer for a falcon already consumed; the gift was given before the request could arrive; Records of Impossible Gifts — Things Given Before the Request Could Arrive |
| BLQ-06 | The Three Descents | 6 | NAP → (galley) → RHD | Authentication through ordeal; letter of credit lost and recovered three times in one Naples night; the seal that holds through three consecutive disasters is more credibly itself; Debt Instruments — Papers That Survived What They Were Sent Through |
| BLQ-07 | The Night Transport | 7 | FLR → FAM → ALE | The document that arrives before the messenger who should carry it; Hospitaller relay creates impossible return journey; letter reaches Florence two days before the deadline; Dispatch Records — Letters Arriving Before Their Preconditions |
| BLQ-08 | The Goosebridge Answer | 8 | CON → RGS → VEN → WM | One document, two incompatible readings, both correct; the ambiguity is the deliberate content of the answer; simultaneous filing creates equal legal standing; Diplomatic Instruments — Responses Whose Completeness Is Invisible Until Both Questioners Have Left the Room |
| BLQ-09 | The Jewel Chest | 9 | BRI → Monopoli → BAR | The object that cannot be opened while carried; multiple claimants, none with the actual right; contents identified by court opening as a third party's estate; Sea-Recovery Records — Estate Effects Delivered Sealed; Contents Identified by Court Opening as Property of Third Party Not Among Claimants |
| BLQ-10 | The Soldier's Testimony | 10 | ALE → FAM | The witness who cannot carry the evidence; objects valuable only because their owner can identify them; identity restored requires evidence to arrive first; Restitution Records — Objects Carried to the Jurisdiction of Their Owner's True Identity |
| BLQ-11 | The Impossible Conditions | 11 | FLR → AVG → MTP → MAR | Impossible conditions that become possible by literal interpretation; the wife set them because they could be met; the conditions are a map not a wall; Conditions Fulfillment — Literal Compliance; Items Located Through Precise Reading of the Original Conditions' Implicit Information |
| BLQ-12 | The Two Friends' Debt | 12 | ROM → ROM | The debt older than the crisis; bill of exchange written for commercial purposes becomes the only alibi; friendship crosses the distance between wealth and ruin; Exoneration Records — False Confessions Rebutted by Antecedent Documentary Alibi |
| LIL | The Company's Letter | 2 | BDX → PER → SRL (Bordeaux Free Company inn → Périgord forest road → Beaulieu-en-Périgord limestone town) | The document creates law between parties who acknowledge no common authority; the routier safe-conduct is the only agreement both sides will honor because the alternative is fire; the carrier is the neutral party neither side could trust their own representative to be |
| CRL-02 | The Archpriest's Certificate | 2 | PER → BDX | The purchased grace cannot be transferred but the precedent it creates can; a reluctant captain needed not a promise but proof such promises are made at all; carrying the evidence of a completed transaction is not the same as carrying a current guarantee — which is sufficient for a man who needs a reason to march |
| CRL-03 | The Archpriest's Receipt | 3 | AVG → PER → BDX | The accounting category chosen for institutional survival — forty thousand crowns recorded as charitable disbursements; accurate in quantity, strategic in classification; the receipt that makes extortion survive into the permanent record as grace; Disbursements — Charitable Payments Made Under Duress; Records Wherein the Category Heading Was Selected for Institutional Survival Rather Than Accuracy |
| CRL-04 | Ruffin's Passport | 4 | BDX → PER → SRL | The authenticated copy legally equivalent to its original reaching a checkpoint whose protocol was written before authenticated copies existed; the gap between legal equivalence and institutional habit; Safe Conduct Records — Certified Copies Whose Legal Equivalence Was Contested at Checkpoint and Established by This Filing |
| CRL-05 | The Knolles Commission | 5 | BDX → PER | The filing location as jurisdictional statement — the English registry mark that answers the question of which law governs a Gascon captain's authority before it can be asked; Commission Records — Royal Authorizations Filed Through Foreign Registries; The Registry of Filing as Jurisdictional Statement |

| CRL-07 | The Jacquerie Account | 7 | PER → BDX | The record preserved against the preserver's judgment — the dying archivist who kept the document of atrocity for nine years because the archive's function has no moral exception; Suppression Records — Emergency Authority Actions; Preserved by the Dying Archivist Who Could Not Refuse Preservation |
| CRL-08 | The Pope's Guarantee | 8 | AVG → PER → BDX | The letter addressed to the dead — the absolution that cannot be delivered but can be filed as evidence of institutional intent; the personal promise transformed into institutional precedent by the death of the promisee; Absolution Records — Letters Addressed to the Dead; Filed as Evidence of Prior Institutional Intent Rather Than Personal Grant |

| BEY-03 | The Odoric Annotations | 3 | VEN → RGS → WM | The source annotated by its derivative; Mandeville's borrowings marked throughout Odoric; the annotated volume proves the compilation was deliberate; evidence of method |
| BEY-04 | The Prester John Letter | 4 | MGZ → RGS → WM | The document that is evidence for what it isn't; the letter is real and dated; it is not evidence that Prester John exists; the archive declines to adjudicate |
| BEY-05 | The Cotton-Lamb Record | 5 | CAF → CON → WM | The administrative debunking; an accountant's invoice note disproves a wonder with more authority than any philosopher's refutation because it was written by someone with nothing to argue |
| BEY-06 | Boldensele's Original | 6 | STR → WM | The original demoted by its own derivative; marginalia no copy preserved; the archive restores priority to the source that Mandeville obscured |
| BEY-07 | The Missing Chapter | 7 | AUG → RGS → WM | The chapter absent from all other copies; the removal is the proof of its significance; the archive records both the chapter and the pattern of its absence |
| BGW | The Caliph's Own Copy | 2 | CAI → ALE → WM (Cairo Sufi hospice → Alexandria Genoese factor's house → Weimar archive) | The margin is the only place the diminished heir of a great institution can speak honestly about what the institution has become; the stories that celebrate the caliphate's height are most revealing when annotated by the man who holds the title with none of the power; what he wrote there he addressed to no one, which is why it is the only honest thing in the record |

---

## Theme Threads Active

- **The grief that has become an identity — the warrior who will not live because living is abandonment; the question that breaks the silence is not whether to live but who will write her name when he is gone; the choice to stay is made not as hope but as the single remaining obligation — to be findable when she is ready to find him**: TBS cycle 2 — a captured warrior in a panther's skin has refused to eat, refused to name himself, refused to speak to anyone except in verses about a woman no one knows; the Fighter is locked in the cell with a blank cedar tablet and one instruction from the physician; over four acts the tablet fills: one fact in the Fighter's hand, her name in the warrior's hand, a direction in the warrior's hand; the guards try to remove him before the last act; at Act V the warrior writes his own name — not because he has hope, but because being findable is the one obligation he can still fulfill; "Take it to the physician. Tell her I will eat tomorrow."
- **Grief that occupies public space asks to be seen before it can be moved: the distinction between the cursed and the broken must be established before the court can act; the inquiry records what the warrior said, not what the court wanted him to be**: TBS cycle 3 — a warrior in black armour has blocked the mountain road crossroads for three days, turning away all who approach; the court wants him declared mad because madness allows a different response than grief does; the Fighter sits down and waits until he speaks; the inquiry records his conditions, his coherence, and the specific distinction the court needed; Sweelinck files it under Grief as Public Obstruction, first entry: "he asked to be seen, not moved"
- **The command document that was simultaneously an act of love and a declaration of war: the only authority available to the imprisoned; consequences intended and irreversible at the moment of sealing**: TBS cycle 4 — a princess imprisoned before an unwanted marriage had one instrument; her sealed letter commanded her champion to kill the groom rather than permit the wedding; the letter created the crime, the war, and the love story simultaneously; the Fighter intercepts it mid-transit and carries it sealed to Aleppo where a legal philosopher reads it as evidence of what authority looks like when it has only one instrument left; Sweelinck files it under Command Documents That Create Their Own Consequences, first entry
- **The person who remains when everyone with better options has left: sustained practical care performed without witness, reward, or certainty; witness as presence**: TBS cycle 5 — Asmat has lived alone at the forest edge for two years, bringing food and news to a grief-mad warrior within; she is not bound by oath, not compelled, not hoping in any ordinary sense; she stayed because someone had to, and she was there; her written vigil record travels to Constantinople and then to Weimar; Sweelinck creates a new category: Solitary Vigil Records — Sustained Witness in Impossible Conditions; "the archive names this as its own kind of heroism"
- **Sworn brotherhood requires each party to name what the oath costs them; what is named in brotherhood is given, not pledged; the oath overrides feudal obligation not because it is higher but because it is different**: TBS cycle 6 — three knights on a headland before an assault each name the most valuable thing they possess as the price of what they are about to ask of the others; Avtandil names his queen's command; Tariel names his grief; Pridon names his kingdom; no one witnessed it and no record exists; the Fighter writes down all three names in order and carries the inscription through Damascus to Weimar; Sweelinck files it under Sworn Brotherhood Records, first entry: "what is named in brotherhood is given, not pledged"
- **The single-use tactical document that succeeded because there was no second plan: the gate held from inside with no exit; one tide, one moment, conditions that will not repeat**: TBS cycle 7 — the demon king of Kajeti's island fortress had never been taken; the plan required someone to hold a narrow gate from inside with no exit strategy; the tide-chart with three annotations (gate, tower stairs, prisoner's window) is the record of how it fell once; the Fighter carries it from Tbilisi to Caffa past a Genoese factor who wants to sell it back to the fortress, secures the captain's navigational notes as attachment, and delivers both to Weimar; Sweelinck files under Tactical Records — Impregnable Fortresses Taken, first entry: "the gate had to hold"
- **Loyalty under the long watch**: LHR, STN, TBS — the Fighter who stays when others leave
- **The sovereign who cannot trust her household asks a stranger: love coded in court protocol; the commissioned observation that can only be honest because the observer owes nothing; the record made without allegiance is the only record the queen can use**: LHR cycle 3 — Wealhtheow presses a blank tablet into the Fighter's hand after the great necklace presentation and gives one instruction: observe who speaks with Hrothulf and write it down; over four acts the tablet fills with three names, three durations, three directions, and the queen's own four-line annotation added before sunrise; the Fighter carries it through a court official's challenge and a Baltic trader's offer, to Weimar; Sweelinck files it as Covert Political Observation Records, first entry: "she asked a stranger because her household had already chosen"
- **The catalytic object that woke dormant consequence: the cup from the barrow; what was sleeping in the mound was not gold but the specific duration of a world; removing the containment is not the same as causing the event**: LHR cycle 4 — a slave took a two-handled gold cup from a three-century hoard because he needed something to trade; the dragon woke; the village burned; the king died; the cup sits in a bog with its handles up as if placed deliberately; the Fighter pulls it out, carries it past an elder's destruction order, a merchant's silver offer, and a scholar's publication request, to Weimar; Sweelinck files it as Catalytic Objects, first entry: "the cup did not cause the catastrophe — it was the specific object around which the catastrophe had been organized"
- **The death dispatch that carried both a death and its consequence: accurate bad news delivered whole; the prediction believed by the people who could act on it is sufficient; the carrier who must find the right order without changing the truth**: LHR cycle 5 — Wiglaf cannot carry the news himself; the Fighter takes the sealed birch-bark dispatch through a patrol that wants to run ahead, a captain who wants to manage the distribution, and three household heads who refuse to believe the war prediction; the two women who believe it are already packing before the assembly ends; they are right; Sweelinck files it as Death Dispatches, first entry: "accurate bad news delivered whole is the only available form of preparation"
- **The grief for a world's end rather than a person's death: the anonymous mourning-woman who sang the future from the past; the elegy addressed not to the dead king but to the living subjects who will survive him**: LHR cycle 6 — the woman appears at the pyre's edge without invitation and sings of evil days, multitudes of slaughters, the terror of troops, humiliation, and captivity; she has seen other kings die; the Fighter writes her words in real time as the fire is lit; Sweelinck creates a new category: Anonymous Elegy Records — Mourning-Women's Songs, first entry: "the elegy for the future is a different document from the elegy for a person"
- **Gift-obligation released through correct speech: the weapon given under shame, returned under courtesy; releasing an obligation does not prevent re-entry; both acts permanent**: LHR cycle 7 — Unferth gave a sword that failed; Beowulf returned it with carefully chosen words of praise; both men know the truth; Unferth wants a record of both speeches so the obligation can be formally closed at a shrine; the Fighter writes both speeches, carries the bond record through a weapons registry checkpoint and a shrine-keeper's counter-claim, and receives the supplement at the harbor: Unferth died carrying Hrunting in the Swedish raid, having taken it back from the shrine; Sweelinck files both: "releasing an obligation does not prevent re-entry"
- **Grief carried in an object**: INV, BHD, GDN — a thing holds the dead
- **Honor vs. shame**: MAD, GDN, BHD — the cost of keeping a vow when the lord breaks it
- **Love and fate intertwined**: MSE, INV, BHD — love that ends in loss but is not diminished by it
- **Truth delivered posthumously**: LGW — a dead woman's words carried through a world that preferred silence
- **The encoded message that uses its own medium: the harp tuned to one word; the carrier who must not translate**: LGW cycle 3 — Tristan tunes his harp so every chord says one thing; the Fighter carries it through a Cornish official who demands a verbal message and a scholar who wants to analyze the tuning, to Weimar; Sweelinck files it as Encoded Message Records, first entry: "the strings are tuned to say one thing; Tristan is still alive"
- **The proxy token that grants what the bearer cannot yet claim: authority carried for a person before they have earned the right to carry it themselves**: LGW cycle 4 — Lynet's silver rod carried for Gareth before he had rank; the Fighter carries it from Camelot through a gate official who demands the knight's own credential and a road captain who wants the knight present, to Weimar; Sweelinck files it as Proxy Authority Records, first entry: "the rod carried what Gareth had not yet earned"
- **The reconciliation letter that reversed years; whether it arrived in time is the war's question, not the archive's**: LGW cycle 5 — Gawain's letter to Lancelot, seal ring still warm; the Fighter carries it from Camlann through a messenger who wants to read it first and a checkpoint that demands an official seal, to Weimar; Sweelinck files it as Reconciliation Records, first entry: "it reverses years; it arrived"
- **The sacred object still warm; the procession occurred; the archive holds the fact and the temperature**: LGW cycle 6 — cloth still warm from the Grail procession; the Fighter carries it from Corbenic through a relic dealer who offers triple and a church official who wants to authenticate it on site, to Weimar; Sweelinck files it as Procession Records, first entry: "the procession occurred; the cloth was present; it is still warm"
- **The apology sent the night before and arrived the day after; the archive holds it for the addressee who is not here to receive it**: LGW cycle 7 — Morgan's apology to Arthur written the night before Camlann, arrived the morning after he died; the Fighter carries it from Avalon through a royal household official who claims the right of first receipt and a courier who wants to deliver it to Guinevere instead, to Weimar; Sweelinck files it as Undelivered Letter Records, first entry: "she sent it the night before; it arrived after; the archive holds it for the king it was addressed to"
- **Healing carried through hostile law**: MAN — medicine that cannot announce its source; the physician who must stay behind
- **The carrier becomes the hunted**: SEN — an object of value marks its bearer; possession turns an innkeeper's boy into prey; delivery and survival are the same act
- **The token's journey**: ALL — the object passes hand to hand; its state marks the story's progress
- **Ancient witnesses and the relay of questions**: KIR — the oldest living things circle the site of a wrong they cannot fix; the Fighter carries a question through the chain until the door at the bottom opens
- **Corruption wears the face of mercy; the genuine article has no price**: FCO — every institutional gate between the Fighter and a condemned man is owned by Lady Mede; the plain pardon from Truth resists purchase because it costs nothing and says only "do well and have well"
- **Fate moves through the broken thing; what carries a future must first appear dead**: FRO — the Shards of Gram are iron that looks like nothing on a blood-dark field; the Fighter guards a future they cannot name, through every gate and every patrol, until the shards reach the hand that will one day forge Gram
- **The greatest man alive has no house: heroism that earns protection for all and shelter for none**: VBY — Grettir clears hauntings, kills monsters, saves farms, swims impossible distances through winter sea; none of it earns him pardon; the law has no mechanism to credit deeds performed outside it; to carry fire for Grettir is to do the same thing he does — care that the law cannot acknowledge
- **The invitation as the weapon: what looks like reconciliation is the shape of vengeance**: LBC — Kriemhild's sealed scroll is a festival invitation and a funeral notice simultaneously; every gate the Fighter carries it through is a gate the Burgundians are already passing through toward their deaths; the weapon is the form itself — hospitality, courtesy, family obligation
- **The poem that ransoms a life: language deployed as the final weapon when all other weapons are gone**: RIX — Egil's axe cannot help him in York; only the poem can stop the axe; the Fighter carries the scroll through Gunnhildr's agents to the hall where it must be heard, because a poem of sufficient quality creates an obligation in the person who receives it
- **The one who will not weep: when a single refusal outweighs the world's willingness**: BOO — Frigg asked everything to weep for Baldr and everything agreed; one voice in one cave said no; the world's entire accumulated grief cannot outweigh it; the Fighter witnesses the refusal and names what it is — a recognition that changes nothing and is the only possible form of success
- **The man who decides the form of his own ending; the witness who holds the space of that decision; the relic that carries forward what the death cannot carry**: BHD (cycle 2) — Cuchulain lashes himself to the standing stone so he will die on his feet; the Fighter cannot stop what is happening; the only act available is holding the boundary exactly as he defined it, and then the retrieval; the commission is not rescue but precision; the sword carried north is not grief but continuation
- **The unwitnessed negotiation that changed everything — the act that resolved the crisis by requiring one party to give up the thing they most valued, with no official record and no acknowledgment; the druids gave one party forgetfulness but not the other**: BHD cycle 3 — Emer tells Fand she will give up Cuchulain if Fand returns to the sea; the druids then give Cuchulain a drink of forgetfulness but do not give one to Emer; the Fighter carries a note written by a woman who was present but unnamed in any official record; Sweelinck creates Unwitnessed Negotiations — Acts That Changed Situations Without Official Record, first entry: "Emer's offer is what made it possible for the story to end; the archive names what was never named"
- **The recognition that arrived correctly and changed nothing; some structures are designed to be entered completely before the recognition is possible; the ring was always on the boy's hand**: BHD cycle 4 — a woman in the Ulster camp recognized Cuchulain's gold thumb-ring on the boy at the ford; the identification note was carried correctly, delivered correctly, read correctly; the combat happened anyway because the instructions the boy received from his father were also correct, and the two correct things could not both be honored simultaneously; Sweelinck files it under Recognition Documents — Identifications That Arrived After the Irreversible Act, first entry: "not a failure of the carrier; evidence that some structures are designed to be entered completely before the recognition is possible"
- **The supply record that became a witness record; three evenings of what correct friendship costs when it becomes correct killing; the grief observed at combat boundaries that no official account preserved**: BHD cycle 5 — the healer Lena sent the cart to the ford each evening with one instruction: write down what happens at dusk; the front of the document is inventory; the back is three evenings of two men sharing food and medicine across the water and saying good night to the person they are about to kill; Sweelinck files the two sides separately: "the healer's cart as the mechanism of access; the back of the document as the archive of what the front could not contain; Witness Records — Grief Observed at Combat Boundaries, first entry"
- **The lament the warrior code does not permit over an enemy who was also a friend; the heroic record and the grief record are not in competition — they are the complete account of a single event, and both are necessary**: BHD cycle 6 — Cuchulain spoke over Ferdiad's body in the ford and named what was lost before the combat was ever a combat; a scald composed a version without the lament because it complicated the clean close; an Ulster counselor wanted it suppressed; the Fighter carried the transcription past both; Sweelinck files it under Combat Elegies — Laments Spoken by Victors Over the Bodies of Friends, first entry: "the archive notes that the heroic record and the grief record are the complete account of a single event"
- **The sustained refusal of normative display as the only form of protest available to the captive; the unsmiling as a document as legible as any signed testimony; the absence on the last day as the final entry**: BHD cycle 7 — Deirdre lived in Conchubar's hall for a year without smiling; the household tried everything; a servant kept the record because the year needed to be recorded; the account is twelve months of observational entries and one absence; Sweelinck writes: Grief Records — Sustained Refusal of Normative Display, first entry: "the refusal to perform grief in the expected manner is the sustained assertion that this hall is not where the person lives, regardless of where the person sleeps"
- **The proxy errand; the commission given by someone who cannot complete it themselves; the marker planted not to celebrate what happened but to record that the world ran its course and someone was present for it**: MSE (cycle 2) — the old man has been trying to die for a very long time and cannot; he can only point others toward it; his walking staff, planted at the oak beside the three dead men, completes an errand he has been carrying at every road junction for longer than the Fighter has been alive; the gold was always Death; they found exactly what they were looking for
- **The act carried out in the absence of legitimate jurisdiction; the witness who carries the evidence without becoming a partisan; the honest account delivered whole to the one who authorized it**: SDQ (cycle 2) — Helen MacGregor executes a government spy at Loch Lomond because the law will not act on her behalf and the threat is immediate; the Fighter witnesses, carries the warrant south through the patrol zone, and delivers an honest account to Rob Roy — neither condemning Helen nor defending her; the account given whole is the only form of fidelity the commission requires
- **The scattered truth; the document that only functions whole; the carrier who reassembles what others have divided without judging the holders who could only see their piece**: FCO (cycle 2) — Piers tore his pardon and the pieces scattered to people who each read their own righteousness into the fragment they hold; the Friar, the Pardoner, and the merchant's steward are not lying — they are reading their piece accurately and ignoring the clause that qualifies the promise; the Fighter retrieves all four pieces without correcting the misreaders directly, assembles the pardon, and delivers it whole; the full text says exactly what it always said and always did
- **The interior route given literal form; the document that cannot be followed unless the follower already knows the way it describes; evidence that the route exists and was given**: FCO (cycle 3) — Piers's pilgrimage directions to Truth: each interior station named as geography, each junction's instruction given, the warnings at Pride and Covetousness, the final gate; Will wrote them as fast as he could; the archive holds them as evidence that the directions were given; Sweelinck opens Pilgrimage Direction Records: "the route exists; the archive cannot walk it for you"
- **The legal argument that established whether mercy was permitted; the brief that authorized the most consequential act by overriding valid objections rather than refuting them**: FCO (cycle 4) — the Four Daughters of God debate before the Harrowing: Truth's three points against, Righteousness's brief, Peace's resolution; the points were not refuted; the framework was overridden; the gate opened; Sweelinck opens Divine Legal Records: "Truth's objections are still valid within their framework; the gate opened anyway"
- **The social document disguised as a confession record; the tavern bill as precise inventory of an afternoon that was supposed to be spent in church**: FCO (cycle 5) — Glutton's alehouse bill from Beton's: itemized, dated, marked at the point where Glutton stopped paying for others; primarily a social record of fourteenth-century English tavern life; Sweelinck opens Confession Evidence Records: "the bill predates the confession by six days; the afternoon is documented in the order things were consumed"
- **The argument that need overrides law; the claim that poverty suspends the conditions under which crime is possible; not refuted before the poem ended**: FCO (cycle 6) — Need's argument to Will: opening claim, three virtue examples, legal citations, conclusion; the argument was not answered before the Antichrist arrived; the archive holds the argument and the non-refutation; Sweelinck opens Necessity Doctrine Records: "Need did not stay to hear the refutation"
- **The open quest; the oath sworn before a destination known; the file the archive holds open because the quest has not concluded**: FCO (cycle 7) — Conscience walks out of besieged Unity crying 'Grace', swearing to find Piers Plowman; destination unknown; Sweelinck opens Open Quest Records and marks the file open: "the archive holds open quests because a quest that has not concluded is still a quest; this is the first entry"
- **The last instruction designed with precision; the dying woman who arranges her own exit; the token placed exactly because the placement is the statement she is still making**: FRO (cycle 2) — Brynhild distributed her gold and dictated her pyre-arrangements with architectural precision; the sword placed between her and Sigurd on the pyre is the same sword that was between them the night they were together correctly; Gunnar tries to prevent it because the placement publicly names what he did; the Fighter carries the sword through five acts of resistance and places it exactly as she specified, because the placement is the only form of correction still available to her
- **The enabling condition; the one thing the hero needs and names with brevity; the light-carrier who makes the heroic act possible by staying still and staying lit**: VBY (cycle 2) — Grettir descends into a Norwegian burial mound for treasure and requires exactly one thing: someone to hold the torch; the Fighter carries the light through five acts of underground combat, a wight's grab at the flame, a collapsing beam, and the final gesture of extinguishing the torch at the barrow threshold where the world's own light begins; the heroism was Grettir's; the enabling condition was not
- **The dying enemy's words that changed the hero's permanent condition; the curse as the precise form of the last thing a defeated man could still do**: VBY (cycle 3) — Glámr spoke the curse before Grettir cut his head off; three witnesses heard it; the farmer of Þórhallsstaðir wrote it down the next morning; the archive wants the exact clause order because the curse is a record of what one man's last strength could do to another man's life; Sweelinck files it as Curse Records, first entry: "the correlation is noted; the archive holds no opinion about the mechanism"
- **The sentence that made all help illegal; the law's instrument that reduces heroism to irrelevance and care to complicity**: VBY (cycle 4) — Grettir's full outlawry sentence: accumulated charges, the term 'skógarmaðr,' the term 'for life,' the clause that made feeding him an offense; the archive holds both the sentence and the heroic deeds separately because both are true about the same man; Sweelinck files it as Outlawry Records, first entry: "the heroic deeds are not in the sentence"
- **The oath that committed its carrier to the end of the world; the document that authorized a journey no law required but family obligation made necessary**: VBY (cycle 5) — Þorsteinn the Gallows-Man swears at the Althing to pursue Grettir's killers wherever they go; the oath names Þuríðr and Narfi; he tracks them to Constantinople and kills Narfi there; the archive holds the authorization; the killing is in the other files; Sweelinck files it as Revenge Commission Records, first entry: "Iceland to Constantinople on a brother's behalf"
- **The sorcery weapon as forensic evidence; the runes that killed the strongest man in Iceland by turning his own strength against him**: VBY (cycle 6) — Þuríðr's rune inscription transcribed before the log was burned; each rune in sequence; the specific inscription that caused Grettir's axe to turn into his own thigh; the Fighter carries it past Þuríðr's kin and a church official's confiscation order; Sweelinck files it as Sorcery Evidence Records, first entry: "the transcription is the record of the method; the method succeeded"
- **The sibling testimony written after the obligation is complete; the account that closes the record of a life the law could not accommodate**: VBY (cycle 7) — Þorsteinn writes his account in Constantinople after the revenge is done: Grettir's death, the sorcery, the journey, the killing; sealed before departure; the archive at Weimar is where the road ends; Sweelinck files it as Sibling Testimony Records, first entry: "Grettir was the strongest man in Iceland and the law had no mechanism for that"
- **The carrier who arrives with a reason to live; the offer made without coercion to a person who has already completed their decision; the witness who holds what they carried without knowing if it will be taken**: GCI (cycle 2) — Gilliatt sits in the Gild-Holm-'Ur tidal seat watching the boat carry Déruchette away; a carrier arrives on the coast path with a message from Lethierry; the message is a reason to come back; Gilliatt does not respond; the carrier holds the message, states it once more, and understands; the spring tide rises; Sweelinck files it as the first record of the archive's most difficult commission — carrying something that could not be refused or accepted, only held
- **The technical record of heroism performed without witnesses; the log that proves what was done by showing how it was done in sequence**: GCI (cycle 3) — Gilliatt's working log from the Douvres salvage: daily entries, tools named, problems solved, the three-day gap when the storm came, the resumed entries, the final line; the archive holds the thinking as evidence of the act; Sweelinck opens Solitary Engineering Records: "he was alone for two months; the log says what he did each day"
- **The careful plan that ended in the rocks; the record of complete competence that was insufficient for one thing the planner did not include**: GCI (cycle 4) — Clubin's scheme notebook recovered from the Durande wreck: grounding plan, tide calculations, money total, prepared identity name; every column correct; the octopus is not in any column; Sweelinck opens Premeditated Wreck Records: "the calculations are correct; the plan was complete up to the moment it was not"
- **The document returned after it was no longer useful to its holder; the last obstacle removed by the person who needed nothing from removing it**: GCI (cycle 5) — Rantaine's signed confession returned through Gilliatt's arrangement; the document changed from leverage to completed transaction; Gilliatt asked nothing; Sweelinck opens Returned Evidence Records: "the archive holds the document and the fact of its return; the how is not in this file"
- **The gratitude that arrived after the person who earned it was no longer able to receive it; the sealed letter on the table in the empty house**: GCI (cycle 6) — Déruchette's letter written before the boat left, delivered after Gilliatt had already gone to the tidal seat; found sealed on his table; the archive holds it sealed; Sweelinck opens Undelivered Gratitude Records: "the archive holds documents; this document's content is not the archive's to read"
- **The last visible act witnessed by the one who had known the person longest; the account written when the witness could write again**: GCI (cycle 7) — Lethierry saw Gilliatt on the quay, saw the direction he walked, understood; wrote the account three days later; the archive holds the account of the last moment before the private; Sweelinck opens Witness Records: "Lethierry was the only one on the quay who knew Gilliatt well enough to understand the direction"
- **The last gift given against the design of every surrounding event; the carrier between two parties who love each other and are about to kill each other; what remains when a good man has been used up by his own honor**: LBC (cycle 2) — Rüdeger cannot refuse Kriemhild because of his oath; he cannot refuse the Burgundians because of his love; both things are simultaneously true and cannot be resolved; the shield exchange is the moment where both truths are acknowledged by both parties; the Fighter carries the shield across the sixty feet of contested ground that both armies have agreed to permit without agreeing to, and holds the neutral ground after Rüdeger falls — Grettir descends into a Norwegian burial mound for treasure and requires exactly one thing: someone to hold the torch; the Fighter carries the light through five acts of underground combat, a wight's grab at the flame, a collapsing beam, and the final gesture of extinguishing the torch at the barrow threshold where the world's own light begins; the heroism was Grettir's; the enabling condition was not — Brynhild distributed her gold and dictated her pyre-arrangements with architectural precision; the sword placed between her and Sigurd on the pyre is the same sword that was between them the night they were together correctly; Gunnar tries to prevent it because the placement publicly names what he did; the Fighter carries the sword through five acts of resistance and places it exactly as she specified, because the placement is the only form of correction still available to her — Piers tore his pardon and the pieces scattered to people who each read their own righteousness into the fragment they hold; the Friar, the Pardoner, and the merchant's steward are not lying — they are reading their piece accurately and ignoring the clause that qualifies the promise; the Fighter retrieves all four pieces without correcting the misreaders directly, assembles the pardon, and delivers it whole; the full text says exactly what it always said and always did — Helen MacGregor executes a government spy at Loch Lomond because the law will not act on her behalf and the threat is immediate; the Fighter witnesses, carries the warrant south through the patrol zone, and delivers an honest account to Rob Roy — neither condemning Helen nor defending her; the account given whole is the only form of fidelity the commission requires — the old man has been trying to die for a very long time and cannot; he can only point others toward it; his walking staff, planted at the oak beside the three dead men, completes an errand he has been carrying at every road junction for longer than the Fighter has been alive; the gold was always Death; they found exactly what they were looking for — Cuchulain lashes himself to the standing stone so he will die on his feet; the Fighter cannot stop what is happening; the only act available is holding the boundary exactly as he defined it, and then the retrieval; the commission is not rescue but precision; the sword carried north is not grief but continuation
- **The certification that holds because the certifiers preferred not to see; the witness record accurate to its face value and wrong at its core; the archive receives the gap between what was done and what was recorded**: LBC cycle 3 — Gunther passed Brunhild's three tests at Ísenlant with Siegfried invisible beside him; the witnesses signed a certification recording what they officially saw; the impossible footprints in the dust beside Gunther's own were not looked at directly by any signatory; the Fighter carries the certification through a scribe who wants to add an accurate margin, a Burgundian knight on the Danube crossing who knows what he chose not to see, and a court herald who asks what is being carried; Sweelinck creates Competition Records — Witnessed Certifications Whose Accuracy Depends on the Witness's Willful Inattention; first entry: "the document is true as written and false as understood"
- **The map disguised as a domestic instruction; the document that was never meant to leave the room; the archive receives the weapon's blueprint rather than the weapon itself**: LBC cycle 4 — Kriemhild wrote a brief note to herself on the sewing table giving the exact placement of the cross she was told would protect Siegfried from the spear; she was not told what it would protect against; the Fighter finds the note before a servant removes it with the linen, carries it past a rider at the Worms gate and a messenger from Hagen on the Danube ferry; Sweelinck creates Vulnerability Records — Instructions That Named the Method; first entry: "the instruction explains what the cross was used for, not what it was said to be"
- **The inventory that survived what it was written to enumerate; the list more permanent than the treasure; the archive holds what the river holds in a different form**: LBC cycle 5 — Hagen threw the Nibelung hoard into the Rhine; one chest did not sink; its waterlogged inventory sheet survives on waxed cloth; the Fighter retrieves it from the current, carries it past Hagen on the bank without showing it, through a Burgundian knight in Cologne who wants to know what it contains, and two Hunnic road agents looking for evidence the hoard was sunk; Sweelinck creates Inventory Records — Surviving Lists for Submerged or Destroyed Wealth; first entry: "the list is the only form in which the hoard persists above the river floor"
- **The vigil record as the only complete evidence of the night where nothing happened; the document created to report the hours in which everything was still possible**: LBC cycle 6 — Volker wrote the nightwatch account on his knee while Hagen held the hall steps at Etzel's court; the Hunnic council was debating in the yard whether to attack now or wait; the document records the exact hours, the position of the guards, the door that did not open; the Fighter receives it without disrupting the silence, exits through the kitchen passage unseen, carries it past a Hunnic captain's curfew check and a Burgundian survivor at dawn; Sweelinck creates Vigil Records — Night Accounts Preceding Known Catastrophes; first entry: "the archive holds what the silence contained"
- **The secret preserved as the concealer's decision rather than as property intelligence; the document that restores Hagen's sovereignty over a choice he made alone by making the choice a record rather than a loss**: LBC cycle 7 — Hagen sank the Nibelung hoard and died without speaking its location; the Fighter was on the boat and knows the exact bend of the Rhine; at Etzel's court Kriemhild's interrogation is answered with nothing useful; standing at the Rhine bank the Fighter decides to sketch the location rather than carry the knowledge into silence; Sweelinck receives the sketch and creates Location Records — Deliberately Concealed Wealth; first entry: "the secret became a document by being written rather than carried into the river; the archive holds the decision, not the treasure"
- **The credential carried before the man it belongs to; the pilgrim who arrives as the sign that prepares the reception; what remains when the feud has run its full course and both men have done their penance**: GDN (cycle 2) — Kari has killed sixteen of Flosi's companions; both walked to Rome; both received absolution; Kari's ship wrecks at Ingolf's Head and he gives the Fighter his pilgrim certificate to carry ahead of him to Swinefell; the Fighter arrives before him and puts the certificate in Flosi's hands before Kari walks through the door; Flosi reads what it says and springs up and kisses the man who enters and the feud that started with a scarlet cloak ends in a handshake
- **The legal technique obtained from the hostile party: the formula extracted by misdirection is valid because legal validity does not depend on the willingness of the party who disclosed it; Njal's scribe wrote it down because a technique that exists belongs to the law, not to the man who tried to keep it**: GDN cycle 3 — Gunnar needed the obsolete dower summons formula to annul Hrut's prior marriage claim over Unn; Hrut had never published it because unpublished it remained a private defense; Gunnar extracted it by misdirection in a sham lawsuit; Njal's scribe recorded the formula the moment Hrut spoke it; the Fighter carries the transcription from Iceland through a ship factor at Birka who recognizes the legal formula's commercial implications, to Sweelinck at Weimar; Sweelinck creates Legal Innovation Records — Techniques Obtained From Hostile Sources, first entry: "Hrut gave it away because he could not refuse to say it in a legal proceeding without losing the case; saying it lost him the case anyway"
- **The prophecy acknowledged in the warned party's own hand: Gunnar wrote his name below Njal's prohibition, which means the archive holds both the warning and the proof that the warned man read it, understood it, and rode back anyway; the document does not explain the return but does make it inexplicable in the only court where it matters**: GDN cycle 4 — Njal told Gunnar that if he broke the exile terms a second time it would cost him his life; Gunnar heard, understood, turned his horse south toward the ships, and then turned back when he saw his home; his acknowledgment of the prohibition is in his own hand; the Fighter carries the vellum from Iceland past Otkell's descendants who want the document's characterization altered, through a Norwegian coastal flood, to Sweelinck at Weimar; Sweelinck creates Prophetic Prohibition Records — Warnings Acknowledged in the Warned Party's Hand, first entry: "the warning and the return are not contradictions — they are the same man's signature on two consecutive acts"
- **The gift whose end was also its completion: Olaf gave Sam knowing the hound would be used exactly once for exactly this purpose; the gift letter and the death account are sewn together because together they describe one complete thing; separating them would make each document less true**: GDN cycle 5 — Olaf Peacock gave Gunnar the hound Sam at the wedding feast with a gift letter describing the animal's qualities; Sam died defending Gunnar, killing two attackers before going down; Grani the thrall wrote the account of Sam's end; a household scribe sewed the gift letter to Grani's account because the combined document was more complete than either alone; the Fighter carries it from Hjardarholt past Olaf's surviving descendants who want the gift letter returned as household property, through an animal-keepers' guild assessment of the fight description, to Sweelinck; Sweelinck creates Gift Records — Objects Whose End Was Also Their Completion, first entry: "the gift was complete when it was used; the letter and the account belong together"
- **The forensic measurement of an act at the limit of the body's capacity: three witnesses measured the twelve-ell leap with a knotted cord the morning after; the saga's number and the measurement's number agree; the archive holds the measurement not because the saga needed corroborating but because the measurement exists and belongs somewhere**: GDN cycle 6 — Skarphedinn's leap across the Markfleet ice with axe raised struck three witnesses as requiring measurement; they knotted a cord that morning and preserved the knot-distance; the measurement is twelve ells, matching the saga's number exactly; the coincidence makes the document harder to classify, not easier; the Fighter carries the cord and witness transcript from Iceland through a saga compiler at Hedeby who wants the measurement integrated into his text, past an athletics judge who doubts the number, to Sweelinck; Sweelinck creates Physical Evidence Records — Forensic Measurements of Acts at the Limit of the Body's Capacity, first entry: "the measurement does not prove the leap; it records that the leap was the kind of act people measured"
