- **The invitation is a form of authorship; Proxenus made the Anabasis possible by writing the letter that brought Xenophon to the expedition; without the letter, the march never happens and the text never exists; the archive creates a category for the document that enabled the document**: NWI cycle 4 — a Byzantine scholar from the Mystras academy published the find three months ago and the Florentine Duke of Athens claims jurisdiction over Boeotian manuscripts; Brother Stavros in the Orchomenos monastery releases the copy to the Fighter; the scholar's priority claim must be navigated; a Duke's library agent arrives at Ragusa; Sweelinck creates Enabling Documents — Letters That Made the Primary Text Possible, first entry: "Proxenus wrote the letter; Xenophon wrote the book; without the letter there is no book"
- **Evidence of loss is a primary document; the catalog entry that names a text that no longer exists is evidence of what was there; the harbor master did not know what he was logging but the log proves the text existed**: NWI cycle 5 — the Varna civic archive holds a harbor master's salvage catalog entry describing seven-sectioned Greek text recovered from a wreck; head clerk Radu treats the archive commission as a commercial inquiry until the Fighter argues custodial authority; a Byzantine archivist on the Black Sea crossing identifies the structural description as one of eight lost classical texts; Sweelinck creates Loss Documentation — Catalog Evidence of Texts No Longer Extant, first entry: "the harbor master described what he could not read; the archive holds what survived the description"
- **The operational record versus the literary account; the day-log records what happened; the Anabasis records what it meant; both are primary and neither is the correction of the other; the archive holds both as equally authoritative**: NWI cycle 6 — a Trebizond monastery library holds the Rearguard's Log, a field officer's daily record of the same events Xenophon describes in Book 3; the events agree; the language is different; a Constantinople classical scholar wants to publish it as a correction to the Anabasis; the Fighter argues it is not a correction but a companion record; Sweelinck creates Parallel Primary Sources, first entry: "the log and the Anabasis are both correct; both are filed here"
- **The diplomatic formula for a sacred oath records the exact form of the betrayal; the oath table preserves the language Tissaphernes used and the language makes the violation visible as deliberate choice, not accident**: NWI cycle 7 — a Venice archive holds a bronze tablet inscribed with the diplomatic formula for the Persian satrap's oath; a Venetian legal scholar wants to use it as evidence in a current Byzantine-Venetian treaty dispute; the Fighter carries it directly to Weimar; Sweelinck creates Treaty Formula Records — Sacred Oath Instruments Documenting Their Own Violation, first entry: "the formula names what was promised; the archive holds the promise; the violation is named by the document itself"
- **The settlement inscription closed the march; the document that ended the Anabasis is the one that made it past tense; the archive that holds the beginning and the march and the end holds the complete arc**: NWI cycle 8 — a monastery recently relocated to Mystras holds a survey transcription of the Scillus grant inscription; Father Demetrios has waited three years for the right archive; an Italian humanist scholar at Ancona wants to publish it as a major classical find; Sweelinck creates Settlement Records — Post-Campaign Documents, first entry: "the start is in the archive; the march is in the archive; the end is in the archive now"
- **The grant document for three towns the army earned and never received; the unfulfilled promise recorded precisely because performance was never demanded; the archive receives the evidence of what was owed alongside the evidence of the settlement that was given**: NWI cycle 9 — Konstantinos Chrysaphes in Constantinople has held the Bisanthe grant parchment for five generations in his family archive; a Turkish property development consortium wants it suppressed; Sweelinck creates Unfulfilled Grant Records — Documented Promises Without Performance, first entry under Greek Mercenary Service Records: "the archive holds what the army was owed; the archive holds where the army settled; both are here now; NWI cycle complete"
- **An army without a patron becomes its own republic; what holds it together is the vote — the willingness of strangers to choose each other when every hierarchy has failed**: NWI — ten thousand Greek soldiers in the middle of Mesopotamia, their generals killed by treachery in a tent, their patron dead, their mission over; before sunrise Xenophon wakes the surviving officers one by one and gives the speech that reconstitutes the army — not by authority he holds but by the argument that waiting to be rescued is also a choice, and a worse one; the soldiers vote; new generals are elected by the governed; the hollow square forms and the march north begins; seventeen hundred years later a Constantinople scholar sends the leaf that describes this moment to the archive of the city the army was looking at when they cried out; the Fighter carries it through a Genoese inspection gate, past a Venetian collector's offer, through a mountain ambush above Trebizond, and into the hands of a terrified deputy archivist who reads the leaf and asks: did they make it all the way home? The token taken: "Most of them."
- **The fire cannot testify to its own origin — only the scroll with names from the first night constitutes evidence of continuous transmission; the community that kept the flame is not the same as the property the waqf court wants to seize**: KYA cycle 3 — Bahram Yazdi at the Yazd fire temple hands over the Fire Chain Scroll before a court agent reaches him; the Fighter carries it past a Muzaffarid checkpoint that wants to classify it as a religious relic, past the Zoroastrian exile who wants a legal copy for his inheritance case, through a waqf courier pursuing the route, to Ahmad al-Farghani in Tabriz who adds authentication without opinion; filed under Zoroastrian Sacred Transmission Records — Fire Custody Chain
- **The oldest writing lesson in the world, or the most accurate 9th-century imagining of the oldest writing lesson in the world; the question is more valuable than either answer, because an archive that ruled prematurely would have been wrong about half the evidence**: KYA cycle 4 — three folios of proto-Pahlavi demonic script with Pahlavi transliteration, acquired by a Nishapur scholar from a dying student; the Ottoman network wants it; Katakalon in Constantinople asks "what changes if we know the answer" and adds "the question is more valuable than either answer"; filed under Teaching Documents Under Duress — Writing Instruction Given as Ransom
- **The chronicle names the queen who was unnamed in the legend and records what she saw: "He stopped asking for counsel and began demanding confirmation" — the supplement is what the hero story had room for only after the hero's story was told**: KYA cycle 5 — a Zábulistán court history from Kabul names Sháhdokht and records her political observation; the Fighter carries it from a retiring historian past a hired translator attempting document substitution, past a collector's formal purchase offer, to Tabriz; filed under Shah-Nameh Supplementary Records — Unnamed Women, Volume I
- **Six dynasties, same phrase, same acknowledgment — "added in acknowledgment of the banner's origin" — the guild ledger is the material record of a tradition that no dynasty could claim to have created**: KYA cycle 6 — the Anjuman-i Zargar guild ledger from Tabriz, seventeen folios of metalwork records for six royal administrations; dying guild master Khalid ibn Ahmad sells it; Genoese dealer wants it for Florence; false chain-of-custody letter names a family not in the records; filed under Derafsh Kaviani Material Records — Guild Ledger of Successive Royal Additions
- **The case has been sealed since the 11th century and the sealed state is the primary archival condition — to open it is to destroy the most important fact about it, which is that it was kept intact**: KYA cycle 7 — an ivory case from the Yazd fire temple contains one feather and a chain-of-custody inventory; Katakalon convenes a three-party debate; on success the case stays sealed; on failure one iridescent feather is found and the question answered; the archive receives whichever result occurs; filed under Precautionary Objects — Sacred Items Preserved Against Unmaterialized Need
- **One hundred camels, salt, payment exact, men quiet, departed before first light, no trouble — the civilian witness records the military event in the vocabulary available to a professional innkeeper with no reason to notice anything unusual**: KYA cycle 8 — the Sístán inn record, 22nd copy, 33rd generation; dying innkeeper considers burning it; the generational cover-lines make it look like folklore at a Timurid checkpoint; a Samarkand compilationist certifies a copy and releases the original; filed under Civilian Witness Records — Military Events
- **The trophy is not of the kill; the trophy is of the grip; Rustem had Afrásiyáb in his hands once and the world did not end, and that fact is more important than whether the kill happened**: KYA cycle 9 — a vellum rubbing of the Sístán shrine inscription, taken before the Mongols destroyed the shrine; the inscription records that Afrásiyáb was in Rustem's grip; the astronomer at Maragha must judge whether it is genuine; a flooded stream tests the vellum on the road north; filed under Material Witness Records — Combat Trophies
- **The bracelet entry was the clerk performing the function of describing what the object did — and what the object did was remember; grief documented as inventory is not lesser grief, it is grief that will survive the archive's next inventory**: KYA cycle 10 — Folio 47 of Rustem's armory inventory, from a Sístán dealer selling folios; Ahmad al-Farghani recognizes the memory function; a Venetian agent's prior option letter is legally moot after sale; filed under Grief Objects — Documented Loss Made Material
- **The botanist confirmed the tree exists and refused to confirm its origin — two independent observations sixty years apart describe the same tree at the Oxus border; the archive files them together because they describe the same tree, not because they agree on what grew it**: KYA cycle 11 — Farhad al-Samarqandi's botanical manuscript from Samarkand, a tree with iridescent blue-green leaves grown where Saiáwush stepped; Khrysaphes in Constantinople adds his independent 1358 observation; the Venetian natural history collector is outrun; filed under Physical Evidence of Legendary Events — botanical documentation
- **The prince put both armies on his walls without comment and made an argument with paint; the palace was razed by the man who had him killed; the argument was also razed; this list is the only evidence the argument was made**: KYA cycle 12 — the Khoten court portrait list, listing both Persian and Túránian heroes without distinction; a Chagatai official tries to tax it as commercial; Mansur ibn Farhad's colleague wants to add interpretive commentary; Sweelinck: "The proposal was not destroyed while this list exists"; filed under Shah-Nameh Supplementary Records — Destroyed Locations
- **The Chinese did not know they were describing a legend — they were describing a king who needed to be fed and returned; the document that escaped the tradition's management is the only surviving outside view**: KYA cycle 13 — Han-format administrative record of Kai-Káús's eagle-throne crash, held by a Nestorian translator in Tabriz for twenty years; Katakalon wants it for his Byzantine-Persian collection; Sweelinck: "The Chinese recorded forty days of hospitality and a diplomatic return"; filed under Foreign Administrative Records — Events Recorded Without Knowledge of Their Legendary Status
- **The cup was melted for coin; the sentence that said what the cup could do was not melted; the power exists here only as a description, and a description of a power is all the archive will ever hold when the object itself is gone**: KYA cycle 14 — a single Ilkhanid treasury folio from Maragha, 1335 dispersal; "said to show the possessor all that transpires in the world; status: melted for coin"; a manuscript dealer's prior-client argument fails the historical provenance test; filed under Jemshíd Documents — Described Powers
- **The ring appeared to Rustem twice and the killing happened anyway; the tradition gave it a third appearance because a ring that stops killings must stop a killing — the function was not complete until it was**: KYA cycle 15 — five folios from a Samarkand continuation tradition (Barzú-Nameh); Ahmad al-Farghani wants to add the ring's chain to the document's margin; a collector at a mountain pass sends hired riders; scribe's note: "Firdusi records two appearances. This folio is the third"; filed under Identification Objects — Objects That Stopped Killing
- **Five mounds in the Caspian foothills, 1289, unusual regularity — the traveler confirmed the mounds are there and refused the identification; the question is correctly preserved; the archive leaves it open with him**: KYA cycle 16 — two folios by Yusuf al-Jilani from an estate lot in Trebizond; Katakalon determines whether to treat it as physical evidence or curiosity; a Venetian traveler tries to establish a prior literary claim to the location; final line: "Whether the local account is accurate, I cannot confirm. The mounds are there"; filed under Physical Evidence of Legendary Events — Unconfirmed Location Records
- **The court clerk noted "the Saiáwush ceremony and the Afrásiyáb ceremony" — he was recording what the object did; what it did was remember; the basin was melted for coin; the notation was not**: KYA cycle 17 — a Samarkand treasury folio, late hand added "The channel was for the blood"; Maragha scholar wants both the entry and the melting receipt kept together; the distinction between historical evidence and administrative closure separates them correctly; filed under Instruments of Historical Symmetry
- **The tree was planted at a conversion and grew five centuries; someone counted forty wagons for the branches and three hundred for the trunk; the scholar who described the felling understood there was no longer a reason to plant another**: KYA cycle 18 — the Kashmar Description, three folios by a 10th-century Yazdi scholar, kept in the Yazd community for five hundred years; a botanist at Maragha wants to add a species identification note; the dimensions and final sentence should stand without a correction; filed under Sacred Trees — Descriptions Surviving Destruction
- **The form was invented by a woman stripped of everything except the fact of her condition; fourteen other women used it before courts that recognized the form without knowing its origin; the form survives because the condition it describes is precise and the condition recurs**: KYA cycle 19 — four-folio scribal adaptation of Maníjeh's letter-form, Nishapur 1247; a Genoese dealer's Florence buyer is a collector not a scholar; the provenance chain is only in the original; filed under Women's Documents — Forms That Survived Their Occasions
- **The commission was precise and the engineering problem was solved; whether the dragon was killed is outside the document's scope; the guild's record ends at delivery**: KYA cycle 20 — the Tabriz metalworkers' blade-cart commission for Isfendiyár, cross-section diagram with blade-angle solution; a Timurid military engineer wants to evaluate the design for field use; a mechanical scholar at Nishapur wants to investigate the bracket mechanism's origin; filed under Military Engineering Records — Legendary Commissions
- **The Símúrgh was specific because the specificity was the point — only this branch from this tree; the forester documented the tree that matched; the archive holds the specification and the reluctance in the same cedar case, unopened since 1289**: KYA cycle 21 — a preserved Kázú-wood branch with documentation tag by Farhad al-Sistani; a botanist at Nishapur confirms the tag matches the Shah-Nameh specification; a collector's hired riders wait at the mountain pass; tag: "Chain: one branch, one tag, one case"; filed under Legendary Material Specifications — Objects Documented at Origin
- **The builder recorded dimensions, blade specifications, eleven days, three workers, and payment received in full; whether he knew what the pits were for is not recorded; the professional record ends at payment; the rest was outside the scope of the commission**: KYA cycle 22 — five folios from the Herat library, seven-pit commission; a Timurid officer inspects Herat library outflows; a Samarkand legal scholar argues historical contractor liability; the jurisdiction no longer exists; the parties are dead; filed under Instruments of Death — Builder's Records Without Moral Commentary
- **The archive cannot confirm whether the goblet is one of the four Alexander took from Dárá's table while disguised as his own envoy; the claim is intact; the verification is not possible; the archive holds the object and the question together**: KYA cycle 23 — a gold goblet with Achaemenid fish relief and Mongol inventory notation from Samarkand; Katakalon has a Hellenistic fragment corroborating four cups; the full Shah-Nameh context in Weimar is more complete than a Byzantine fragment; filed under Objects of Contested Provenance — Claims That Cannot Be Verified or Denied
- **The wall is attested in two literary traditions and reported still standing in 1341; the archive holds one stone; the wall holds the rest — the stone and the note are the archive's portion of a structure it cannot possess**: KYA cycle 24 — a limestone block from the Caucasus wall with ΑΛΕΞ and eagle-and-star device, carried by an Armenian merchant from Trebizond; Katakalon writes a final attestation citing the Byzantine Caspian Gate tradition; the merchant's tag: "The eagle is on most of the stones I looked at"; filed under Physical Evidence of Legendary Events — Objects Removed from Standing Structures
- **The satire is the part of the Shah-Nameh that Mahmúd did not receive; the anger was about the thirty years, not the silver; the archive holds the work and the complaint — that is what an archive should hold**: KYA cycle 25 — eight-folio pamphlet copy of Firdusi's satire, Nishapur private copy with colophon by a man who knew Firdusi; Ahmad al-Farghani's final authentication: "The satire is the part of the Shah-Nameh Mahmúd did not receive"; Sweelinck: "The archive holds twenty-five documents from the Shah-Nameh cycle. Keep the anger"; filed under Author's Complaints — Works Where the Creation and the Grievance Are the Same Document
- **The naming that was sworn before the death: the Sibyl's prophecy constitutes founding geographic record because it predated any administrative authority over the named location; a duke can rename what he administers, but he cannot revise a naming that existed before his authority over the place did; the navigator's only inheritance is the cape's name, and the document that carries it is older than the dispute that threatens it; the archive creates a new category — Divine Naming Category — to receive it**: FCO (II) cycle 2 — Palinurus was thrown overboard by Sleep so the fleet could land; he received one thing in return — the Sibyl's word that his headland would carry his name; a duke in 1367 is renaming the cape on new maritime charts; Carmela di Cumae, sixth keeper in an unbroken informal line, holds the wax tablet; the Fighter carries it from the Sibyl's vault archive through a coast-road toll point, to the harbor pilots at Cape Palinurus who have used the old name for eighty years and will press their guild seal beside the Sibyl's, through an ambush by the duke's informal agents, to the Naples Maritime Registry where Ser Matteo must decide whether prophetic geographic record predates administrative authority; it does; the registry shows what the place was called first, and by what authority
- **The enemy made your monument: the record of grief is preserved by those who caused it; the Carthaginian priest who commissioned murals of Trojan suffering in Juno's temple was no friend to Troy, but the commission document survives as the world's most precise proof that lacrimae rerum — the world's capacity for tears — belongs to no side; without the enemy's archival act, the subject would not be in the record at all**: FCO (II) — the Mural Brief is a Punic-Latin bilingual commission document, found in a cedar box under the floor of a ruined scriptorium near Cumae, specifying each scene of Trojan suffering to be painted and ending with the clause "so that all who enter may know the world contains tears for things"; the Fighter carries it past a Genoese merchant's prior-claim bluff, a bishop's agent who wants to use it as theological evidence, a spring Rhine flood, and to the Archivus who must create a new filing category — Acts of Unwilling Witness — to receive it; token taken: "Without them, you wouldn't be in the record" — Odysseus, tied to the mast with wax stopping his crew's ears, heard the Sirens promise him all the truth of the world; he strained at his bonds until they cut him and his men rowed harder; afterward a professional scribe took his dictation at Alcinous's court, noting both what he said and the places where he said he could not continue in the usual form; the tablets have been in the scribe's storage since, making him uneasy, because he has been reading them as if they were the song rather than the bound man's account of the song; the Fighter carries them past a sophist who wants to copy them for his school without understanding what they are, past a sailor who wants to hear what he missed that day and cannot be given what he's asking for, through a dock in chaos, to the Archivus who files them under Conditional Records — Incomplete Transmissions; the token taken: "The gaps are not failures" — Idaeus the old herald drove the mule cart through the sleeping Greek camp and waited with the horses while Priam clasped Achilles's knees and both men wept and then ate together; he has been carrying the formal commission tablet since that night, trying to explain its significance to Trojan survivors who only want to know if there is food; the Fighter carries it from the refugee camp outside Troy's ruins, past a Greek checkpoint officer who wants to catalogue it as war-spoil, past a Greek captain who wants to misuse it as evidence in an honor-debt claim, through a river ford that cannot be avoided, to the Archivus who files it not under Destruction Events but under Diplomatic Records — Priam of Troy: last known official act; the token taken: "This was the last night Troy was Troy" — Arsetes, the Ethiopian eunuch who carried infant Clorinda from her mother's court into exile, had one charge: baptize the child and deliver the queen's votive letter to the St. George shrine; he deferred both for thirty years because he could not bring himself to complete either without losing the life he had built around them; Clorinda was finally baptized by the hand that killed her — the saint's protection was complete; Arsetes is dying in a Christian hospice with the letter still in the pouch; the Fighter carries it from his deathbed, past a Crusade records officer who wants to confiscate it as foreign materiel, past a crusader lord's servants who want it as a relic, through a toll gate that has no exception for sacred donations, to the shrine's keeper of records who opens the thirty-year-old entry marked "awaited" and writes a line through it; the token taken: "She was kept" — the acknowledgment that the contract was already complete, and the payment arrives as receipt not as fulfillment — Pier della Vigna, Frederick II's chief minister, stands in the Wood of Suicides in Hell and swears he was innocent of the treason charges; the human courts convicted him on fabricated evidence composed by rival courtiers; for fifty years a sealed clerk's deposition naming those fabricators has been hidden under the floor of a monastery now being dissolved by papal order; the dissolution threatens the document's legal status and its physical location simultaneously; the Fighter carries it from the dissolving monastery through a papal courier's road-gate, through a restricted archive intake opposed by a notary with a papal writ, through a harbor-district fire blocking the registrar's office, to the Weimar archive where the Archivus must choose general or restricted filing — one of the cardinal's own great-grandfathers is named inside; the token taken: "Both things are true" — divine judgment and human record can diverge, and both persist

## Location Grid — City Weave

Read `../story.md` for full city aesthetics before placing a quest act.
Game cities are canonical. Source-book settings are proposed new nodes.

### Game Cities (Canonical)

| City/Node | Code | Act | Texture | Theme Affinity |
|-----------|------|-----|---------|----------------|
| Birka | BK/CI/IN/CY | I + VIII | Nordic timber, frost, merchant smells, void undercity below | Commerce, loyalty, cold, the Shattered Codex |
| Corelli Quarter | CQ | — | Cobblestone alley, canal-water, cat-haunted rubble | Grief, corruption, old money |
| Fishmonger's Row | FR | — | Ruins, salt, seagull-cry, nets in rubble | Grief, witnessing, loss |
| Civil District | CI | I | Bureaucracy, candlelight, ink-stained stone | Obligation, patience, power through paperwork |
| Neon Undercity | CY | — | Void-lit corridors, mechanical hum, data wraiths | Corruption, transformation, the uncanny |
| Tilbury | TL/DK/SF | II | Harbor, rope-and-salt, sea-access, Magistra Muffat | Maritime, arrival, smuggled things |
| Visby | VS/GC/VC | V | Underground markets, stone cold, Warlord Mordus | Danger, underground trade, dark loyalties |
| Weimar | WM/SQ/MT | VI | Archive-haunted, scholarly, high-church, Archivus Sweelinck | Knowledge, investigation, the void's paper trail |

### Source-Book Nodes (Proposed)

| Source Location | Proposed Node | Terrain | Theme Affinity |
|----------------|---------------|---------|----------------|
| Heorot / monster-mere | HEO | Northern coast, mead-hall gold, bone and smoke | Loyalty, fame, grief, the long watch |
| Beowulf's Barrow headland | BRW | Cliff above the northern sea, low-ceilinged limestone cave, dragon's coils on hoard-gold, two iron rings on stone | The oath paid in fire, the survivor who carries the witness, the gold buried useless again |
| Geatland Coastal Settlement | GEA | A Scandinavian harbor village on the southern Swedish coast after the dragon's burning: three collapsed halls still smearing the sky, surviving boat-house and harbor intact, the elders' assembly point at the long-hall's undamaged end; the village that survived its king and is not yet sure what that means | The aftermath of catastrophe, the community that survives the king who survived for it, the dispatches that carry bad news whole |
| Danish Coastal Port — Lejre Harbor District | DAN | The waterfront below Lejre: a shrine to Danish warrior-ancestors at the end of the dock road; salt-timber, Geat and Danish ships alternating at berths; a Danish court checkpoint on the road from Heorot; the commercial and spiritual node connecting Heorot's patronage economy to Baltic trade and the record-keeping world beyond | Gift-obligation, shrine-keeping, the sword's afterlife, formal release of social bonds |
| Scottish Highland | HLD | Fog, bog, granite and heather, bard-song | Grief, warrior code, sacred oath |
| Ulster coast / Emain Macha | ULC | Stone-ringed dun, sea-crossing, betrayal | Oath-breaking, love's cost |
| Emer's Hall — Ulster plain | EMR | A stone-and-timber hall at the edge of Ulster's central plain: walled court, weaving room, the herb-garden Emer tends herself; the hall the Fighter finds when the dun at Emain Macha will not receive the question; the place where decisions are made without witnesses and without official record | Unwitnessed negotiation, love that gives itself away, the act that changed everything and was never named |
| Athens tournament field | ATH | Tournament dust, temple smoke, noble grief | Fate, love, the tournament of death |
| Sherwood Forest | SHW | Leaf-shadow, arrow-flight, yeoman warmth | Loyalty in small things, disguise |
| Nottingham common — gallows-tree | NGM | The public execution ground outside Nottingham's east gate: permanent scaffold visible from the town wall, open road between treeline and gatehouse, a crowd that always gathers for hangings | Public spectacle, disguise under scrutiny, the bluff that has seconds to hold |
| Templestowe Preceptory | TPR | A fortified Yorkshire Templar compound: stone cloister, cell blocks, a trial hall with gallery, the lists in the outer yard; cold even in summer, smelling of stone and wax and old obligation | Institutional authority, suppressed evidence, bureaucratic self-correction |
| Gild-Holm-'Ur Seat | GHL | A tidal rock niche on Guernsey's western coast face: barely wide enough for shoulders, facing open sea, covered by spring tide; the ledge above is the last place a decision can be made; the sea sound here is constant | Completed lives, the ethics of the offer, the weight of a reason to continue |
| Irish shore after the battle | BRC | A flat Irish strand after a total war: seven survivors in the after-silence of two islands destroyed; the smell of salt and iron | Completion after destruction, the last things carrying the last important thing |
| Harlech promontory | HLG | A Welsh coastal promontory: flat-topped stone, the enchanted feast-ground where Rhiannon's birds sing over the sea and time slows | Enchanted time, the watch-keeper's vigil, grief held at bay |
| Gwales / Grassholm Island | GWL | An island off Pembrokeshire: a stone hall with a forbidden door facing Cornwall; eighty years of contentment and one door that must not be opened | Deferred grief, the forbidden room, protection that becomes delay |
| Gwynvryn — the White Hill | LDN | A low hill above the Thames: the burial mound, the river below, the eastern sky open; the place where the journey ends | Memorial completion, the protective burial, the view toward France |
| Vale of Cona | CNA | A Highland valley with a low brook in autumn: flat pale stones in the water, grass banks, thin tree cover, the specific quality of light in a dale that has seen too many of these afternoons | The dying instruction, the exact service, the refusal that is also a boundary |
| Pillar Stone, Ulster plain | PSU | A single grey standing stone on flat open plain southeast of Emain Macha: taller than a man, faint reddish stain at its base that is not from weather, the field open to the horizon in every direction; eastern treeline where enemies wait; the road north visible from the stone's shadow; ravens at altitude most afternoons; the stillness here knows what it has hosted | Willed completion, the vigil that honors rather than intervenes, the weapon recovered as continuation not trophy, the boundary between a dying man's authority and what comes after |
| Flemish tavern outskirts — plague summer | TVO | A road junction at the edge of a plague-summer Flemish town: one tavern at the left, the main road straight ahead, the old man's milestone at the junction's corner; the funeral bell still audible from inside town; hot and still; the kind of afternoon that has been this temperature since before you remember | The failed errand handed on, the commission given at road junctions, the world that keeps running its course and asking for witnesses |
| Loch Lomond shore — execution site | LLM | A grey Highland loch shore in morning cold: flat stones at the water's edge, the loch wide and still, the treeline ten steps behind; the specific silence of water that has just received something; a hat floating at the edge of the reed-bank | The act carried out where no institution has reach, the witness's position at the water's edge, the document that explains what was at stake |
| Gunnar's mead-hall | GNH | A great Nibelung hall in the old Nordic register: carved beams, firepit, the smell of wood-smoke and old wool; Brynhild's chamber at the far end, sword on the wall above the bed; the hall wrong in the way halls become wrong when someone has decided to die; Gunnar occupying the center with the expression of a man whose accounting has arrived | The designed death, the last distribution, the authority of the dying person that supersedes the household's preference |
| Ingolf's Head wreck site | IGH | A rocky Iceland coastal bay after a winter shipwreck: the ship at the bottom, wreckage on the rocks, the storm still running from the west; Kari and his companions standing in the wind with what they saved; two hours' walk from Swinefell in the direction of everything that ended | The accounting completed, the post-vengeance threshold, the moment before the last walk |
| Swinefell hall | SWF | Flosi's farmstead in the Icelandic interior: a long Norse hall, Flosi's high seat at the center, the fire low in winter, the hall staff who have all heard the name Kari; when the door opens in the story's final pages it lets in the storm and then closes on the handshake that the saga has been building toward for three generations | The feud's end, the equal pilgrims, the door that can be opened or kept closed, the acknowledgment that both men did what they had to do |
| Between-armies contested ground, Etzel's hall | BTL | Sixty feet of open ground between the Burgundian line and the Hunnic line at Etzel's court: stone pavement, three wounded men pulled to the sides, blood on the stone, both armies watching from the perimeter; the specific stillness of a space that everyone has agreed to permit without any agreement being made; what happens here is observed by every party and belongs to none of them | The last gift crossing the space where love and obligation cannot coexist; the neutral carrier; the space where the poem's grief is made visible |
| Burial mound, Norwegian coastal headland | BWH | A grass-covered hill above the Norwegian coast: the stone entrance-post half-buried in heather, the entrance seal cut and badly re-tied; below the threshold: old wood framing, dry stone, the specific darkness of a room that has been closed since before the country knew it was a country; ravens visible from the mound-top at dawn | The dead's space, the enabling condition of heroism, the darkness that requires a light-carrier to be navigable |
| Funeral pyre ground, Gunnar's hall | GNP | The open ground behind Gunnar's hall: the pyre built of oak and ash, sixty feet away from the hall doors; the pyre-master's tools stacked at the side; the crowd of household members arranged at a respectful distance; the two biers waiting with the bodies; the space between them that must be filled correctly before the fire is set | The public statement made in the language of placement; the pyre that is also a record; the fire that burns Gunnar's story alongside Brynhild's story without choosing between them |
| Malvern plowing field, edge | MGF | An open field on the Malvern slopes, empty after the pilgrims have left: plowed ridges still visible, Piers's hearthstone at the cottage at the field's lower edge, the widow's garden visible from the road; the specific quality of a place where something important happened and all the evidence is now scattered | The origin of scattered truth, the place where the fragment was first held correctly, the field that produced the document by producing the man who tore it |
| Glen shelter — Rob Roy's hiding place | GLN | A rock hollow in the Highland interior, an hour's hard walk from any patrol road: one fire, two men, the sound of water in the glen below; Rob Roy larger in person than the stories prepare you for; the quality of stillness of a man who has been hiding for twenty years and is very good at it | The receipt of honest testimony, the account given whole, the man who hears what his wife did without needing it translated |
| Oak at the Flemish crossroads | OKD | A very old oak at a road junction an hour's walk from the tavern town: wide, rooted deep, the grass under it always slightly different from the grass beside it; eight bushels of gold coin scattered in the roots when the right people arrive; the specific quality of stillness that belongs to a place that has been here for this purpose longer than the road; ravens visible from here too | Death in the form of something desired, the transaction already decided before the participants arrive, the marker planted to record that the world ran its course |
| Iceland / Thingvellir | ISL | Fog, blood on stone, silence | Vengeance, the weight of objects |
| Castilian road / Toledo | IBR | Dust, court protocol, beard-honor | Pride, humiliation, long patience |
| Raquel and Vidas' Counting House, Burgos | RVH | A narrow house on the commercial row in Burgos: iron-bound strongboxes on the floor, a table with figures and candles, two locked iron-bound chests in the corner that the moneylenders are forbidden to open; the loan receipt on the table, both signatures already dry; the specific silence of a room where a decision has just been made that everyone in it understands differently | The provisional debt, the collateral that is not what it appears to be, the room where the obligation is entered and the room where it is closed are the same room |
| Monastery of San Pedro de Cardeña | CDN | A Benedictine monastery five leagues south of Burgos: stone walls and an oak-shadowed outer yard, the Cid's sixty men camped in the fields beyond the gate, the church where Ximena and the daughters will be left behind; cold stone and candle-wax and the specific quality of a place that keeps faith with people the world has decided to abandon; the Cid sitting upright on Bavieca's saddle in the pre-dawn dark, waiting for the coin and the receipt | The first night of exile, the provision that makes departure possible, the last place that will receive the dead man's body on the return journey |
| Valencia, City and Palace | VLC | The walled city on the Spanish Mediterranean coast that the Cid took after a months-long siege and governed with such equity that a Moorish scholar voluntarily converted; the high tower where Ximena stood with the daughters at the first sight of the great Moorish army; the palace district where the caged lion escaped; the gate of Roseros facing Castile through which the midnight garrison departed; the smell of the sea and the specific silence of a city held by a man who intended to be here permanently | The governed city, the achieved inheritance, the high tower as witness-point, the midnight gate as the moment the commission ends and the legend begins |
| Georgian mountain road | GEO | Shale, amber and gold, champion's fire | Duty, love, the commission |
| Scottish Highlands / Glasgow | SCT | Heather, Highland fog, Lowland commerce | Loyalty, divided allegiance |
| Osbaldistone Hall — Northumberland Manor | OBH | A dark Jacobite-contested English manor in the northern uplands: stone rooms, a library where political secrets arrive disguised as family correspondence, a garden that is watched from the road; the house of a family divided between loyalty and interest; the specific quiet of a house that knows it is under surveillance | Contested loyalty, the sealed document as the last instrument available to the constrained, the document prepared in conditions of observation |
| Glasgow — Scottish Commercial City | GLA | Glasgow in 1715: the Gallowgate and its painted signs, the counting-houses behind locked doors, the magistrate's house off the Saltmarket with its good fire and its ledgers; a city that is both Lowland Scotland and something larger, with Genoese and Dutch and English commercial connections; the smell of coal smoke and river-water and trade-ink; a city where a magistrate can be Rob Roy's kinsman without anyone finding the contradiction particularly unusual | Commercial attestation, the magistrate as both law and family, the factor's desk as the final delivery point, the Glasgow counting-house as a moral institution |
| Aberfoyle — Highland Gateway Inn | ABF | The Clachan of Aberfoil in 1715: a Highland inn at the threshold of MacGregor territory, stone floor, fire with a plow-blade leaning against the grate, the smell of whisky and horses and peat smoke; the village where the legal world of the Lowlands and the customary world of the Highlands negotiate their meeting; the ford above the village where the Forth can be crossed by a man with his hands bound | The contested threshold, the inn where law and lawlessness share a roof, the ford as the specific geography of escape |
| Highland Glen — Rob Roy's Territory | GLN | A sheltered glen in Highland Perthshire, Rob Roy MacGregor's operational ground: alder and birch in the valley bottom, stone overhang above, no road that government horses can follow; the smell of mountain water and cold stone; a man in this location is either Rob Roy's guest or nobody's problem; the specific quality of a place that is safe precisely because it is not accessible | Highland sovereignty, the safe space defined by inaccessibility, the commission given in a place without witnesses, the verbal token as the only currency that works here |
| Loch Lomond Shore — MacGregor Territory | LLM | The western shore of Loch Lomond at dawn: grey water, mountains behind, the clans assembling in the mist; the place where the Jacobite Rising becomes physical; the place where Helen MacGregor stands with the warrant; the place where names go on rolls and the gap between witness and participant has no legal infrastructure | The political event as unresolvable fact, the execution without legitimate jurisdiction, the muster as the physical form of an uprising, the document that must distinguish observation from action |
| Edinburgh — Scottish Royal Capital | EDI | Edinburgh in 1715 under Hanoverian administration: the High Street and the Canongate, the customs house and the tribunal rooms, the castle above and the closes below; a city where the Rising's aftermath is processed through administrative paperwork; the Royal Mile as the route that passes government and underground Jacobite sympathy within a hundred yards of each other | Government record-making, the tribunal that wants to convert witness testimony into prosecution evidence, the administrative machine that processes loyalty under duress, the closes below the castle as the route that loses followers |
| Astolat riverside manor | AST | Grey morning water, dock on still river, lanterns in willows | Honest love, grief, the thing unsaid while alive |
| Camlann vale | CAM | A flat vale after a large battle: the dead in the shapes of their last effort, one surviving fire, the sky beginning to pale; the geography of an ending that was also an accident | The world after the world, the dying king still speaking clearly, the last commission |
| Lakeside — Camlann | LKS | Black still water, reeds, the exact shore from which a sword was thrown and a hand rose; a barge that departed and did not return | The threshold between the world and what comes after; the act that closes a dispensation |
| River ford passage | RVP | Shallow ford, fast current, willow banks, midnight crossing | Contested passage, loyalty between rival claims |
| Ashby tournament grounds | AHB | Tournament field, Norman heraldry, armorer yards and pavilion streets | Identity concealed under competition; Norman power on display |
| Norman road checkpoint | NRG | Raised road, temporary timber gate, torchlight, cold flat country | Institutional authority at the threshold; contested passage |
| Rotherwood — Cedric's Saxon hall | ROT | Timber-and-rushes, oversized fire, Saxon pride, northern frost | Stubbornness as shelter; the Saxon holdout; old trust new necessity |
| Admiral Benbow Inn | ADM | Chalk cliff coastal village, dark sea below, lantern-lit common room, chalk road running inland | Arrival/departure, danger in ordinary places, the catalyst |
| Tilford Hall gate | TLF | Hampshire manor gate in October — the same gate the Company left through; a woman with a ledger under her left arm, transferred when she heard horses; the road south still visible in both directions | Return, the private reunion, the private object restored, the campaign resolved in one exchange |
| Najera — White Company camp | NAJ | The Spanish plain at the field's edge, night before battle: low fires, fletching archers, the enemy's position visible as distant lights; the next morning: dust and noise and the specific press of ten thousand men deciding which way to run | The campaign's end, the wound in the middle of the victory, the private object at the worst possible moment |
| St. Peter Port, Guernsey harbor | STP | Granite quays, Norman French register, salt fog, fishing boats, tidal sea, English and French law blending | Sea epic, self-sacrifice, unwitnessed heroism |
| Welsh mountain valley | CWT | Shale and oak, morning mist, cold river, one old stone bridge, no people | Ancient witnesses, deep time, the chain of testimony |
| Redynvre hillside | RDV | Two dead forest-cycles visible in the stumps, one surviving great oak, exposed shale ridges | Grief held in landscape, the single survivor |
| Cwm Cawlwd valley | CWL | Steep-sided valley, three forest generations in the geology, dead elm at the crook, no paths | Accumulated witness, silence as language |
| Sigmund's Fallen Field | SFJ | Coastal battlefield, frost-grey, dead men scattered, thorn-hedges at the edge, timber gate road running to the shore, ravens beginning at the margins | Fate, the broken thing, the future hidden in ruin |
| Reykjaness Shore-Farm | RKN | Low stone farmhouse at the edge of winter sea, frost on the thatch, salt in everything, coastal road above the cliff, steep narrow path down to grey stones and grey water | Outlaw's threshold, heroism outside the law, the act that cannot be reported |
| Etzel's Court | ETZ | The great hall of Attila's palace on the Hungarian plain, gold-stitched hangings, smell of horse and felt from the steppe, Kriemhild's presence pressing in every room | Vengeance wearing courtesy's form, power at the world's edge, the long calculation |
| Ísenlant — Brunhild's Island Fortress | NIL | A cold island hall beyond the northern sea: Brunhild's three contest grounds — javelin field, stone ring, long-jump mark — the champion's stand, the witnesses watching from the perimeter; the dust where impossible footprints do not officially appear | The test that cannot be what it was, the certification that holds because the certifiers agreed not to see, the edge of the world where deception produces a marriage |
| Worms — Burgundian Court | WOR | The royal court at Worms on the Rhine, 1200: the hall where Kriemhild grew up, the church steps where the queens quarreled, the sewing room where the tunic was prepared, the gate where riders go and come at all hours; a court organized around the specific catastrophe it is producing | The domestic space of vengeance, the sewing room as the weapon's preparation site, the court that cannot see what it is doing to itself |
| Rhine River Bank — Hoard Crossing | RHN | The Rhine at the bend where Hagen's boat stopped, night: black water, specific current, the exact depth where the chest went down; the bank with one rowan leaning east and the road thirty yards above; the place where the largest secret in the poem is not spoken | Deliberately concealed wealth, the decision made in the dark, the inventory that floated free, the bank where the choice between silence and record is made |
| Cologne — Rhenish Trade City | KOL | Cologne in the medieval Rhineland: the Rhenish road north from the Rhine crossing, cathedral construction visible from a distance, merchants' houses on the river road, the sound of iron work and river commerce; the first northern waystation after the Rhine crossing | The trade route north, the knight who wants to know what was in the chest, the city between the Rhine secret and the northern archive |
| Hunnic Road Settlement | HST | A waystation on the road through Hunnic-controlled territory between Etzel's court and the Rhine: a single inn, Hunnic riders conducting nightwatch, the road monitored at dawn and dusk, the specific silence of a settlement that knows what happened at the court | The road that costs passage, the night-watch crossing point, the first waystation where the document begins its northern journey |
| Danube Ferry Crossing | DBN | A wide grey river in cold morning light, one flat-bottomed ferry worked by one old man who has seen too many couriers, east bank behind, west bank ahead | The point of no return, the river that separates knowing from acting |
| York — Eirik's Hall | YRK | Viking court of Jórvík: stone corridors, torch-smoke, sea-city cold, a great hall at the corridor's end, Gunnhildr's agents in every passage | Language as power, the poem under guard, the king who cannot refuse what art asks |
| Ásgarðr — Frigg's Hall | ASG | A high hall above everything, still with grief, pyre-smoke in the air, the gods standing in silence, the center of what the cosmos has lost | Grief as commission, the witness sent where gods cannot go, the impossible bowl |
| Þökk's Cave | THK | A low cave in a cliff-face north of the mountain pass, a small fire, a grey giantess waiting with composed stillness for a specific visitor | The single refusal, the voice that outweighs the world, recognition that cannot be acted on |
| Hnitbjörg — Suttungr's Vault | HNJ | A granite mountainside above the pine-line, a clean dark drill-hole in the stone, coast road below, the smell of deep mountain and cold air before dawn | The vigil at the extraction point, the guaranteed exit route, the three nights of maintenance and one second of everything |
| Kullervo's Forest | KVF | A pine clearing deep in the Finnish forest, late afternoon light through the canopy, the clearing where a man and his sword held a brief conversation about what it would do | The errand of removal, the moment before turning away, the sword standing upright in the earth |
| Kullervo's Master's Hall | KVM | A Finnish lord's timbered hall, gate and steward, a fire inside, a master who understands what a sword returned without its owner means | The delivery of exactly six words, the master's silence, the shape a man chose for his ending |
| Ægir's Hall | AEG | The sea-god's great feast-hall, torches and salt-water stone, gods in uproar, the threshold where Loki turned and dropped something on the way out | The inside of the divine insult-structure, the feast that became an incident, the object on the floor that no one else noticed |
| Bethune Convent | BTH | A walled convent in northern France, the garden gate, a frightened woman who knows her name is on Milady's list and does not have time to say goodbye | The warning before it is too late; the window measured in hours |
| Relay Post Town | REL | A small post-town twelve miles south of Bethune, a postmaster who left for an emergency, an innkeeper's son with a fast horse and a willingness to be talked into an urgent errand | The letter that must leave in thirty minutes or the junction is missed |
| D'Artagnan's Road Junction | DAR | The road junction where D'Artagnan's southern route intersects the north road to Bethune, an innkeeper's son with a letter, a Fighter arriving on foot from behind | The moment the warning arrives; the four seconds it takes to read; the horse turning north |
| Frankish Families' Gathering | FRS | A road-town inn below the Pyrenean pass, twelve families assembled around a table, the crown official's letter already laid out, waiting for the complete list to arrive before either is opened | The complete versus the official; the names read aloud; the moment the families know what the difference means |
| Chapel near Mazovian Border | CHP | A small chapel room near the Mazovian border, tallow candle, a hospital brother sitting with a folded piece of vellum on the table, eleven days of not knowing who to trust | The record of a last moment, given to the right carrier after careful consideration |
| Danish Shore — Departure Point | DNS | The Danish coast harbor where Fengo's courier party boards for Britain; the tablet sealed and committed before anyone knows the crossing will change its contents | The commission as given, before the sea changes it |
| British King's Court | BRK | The British king's reception hall, coastal fortress, harbor approach, checkpoint gate, and the corridor where the attendant makes his last calculation | The commission completed; the position held; the courier who delivered a sealed document |
| Varangian Barracks, Constantinople | VRG | The Varangian Guard's barracks at the Byzantine capital, harbor smell, late afternoon, the finest warriors in the world in the adjacent rooms | The commission given by a celebrated man who cannot carry his own question; the seal pressed warm |
| Baltic Trading Port | BLT | A Norse trading post on the Baltic route, tavern on the harbor, merchants who move between Birka and Constantinople and know what Varangian seals mean in family-history terms | The cargo of private correspondence, the man who wants information that isn't his |
| Helgafell Hermit Cell | HFG | A small cold cell on the north side of Helgafell, a slot-window, a small fire, an old woman with a book in her lap and fifteen years of waiting | The question delivered; the carrier who must leave before the answer |
| Ring's Private Chamber | RNG | The dying king's inner chamber, a low fire, no court admitted, the signet ring on his forefinger, four sentences to be spoken once | The word the dying man cannot carry himself, the narrow window before the chamberlain enters |
| Ingeborg's Chamber | ING | Ingeborg's private rooms, a writing board, a window, morning light, a woman who has been waiting all morning for news that was not coming through the right channels | The recognition delivered without transformation, the room where the chamberlain's version will arrive second |
| Road-Side Standing Stone | RSS | The standing stone at the edge of Ásgarðr's approach road, old grass at its base, other deposited objects beside it: a rib-bone, a vellum, two chips of various materials | Things too dangerous to keep but too true to destroy; archived truths waiting for the world to decide |
| Eclipse Witness's Farm | ECF | A coastal farmstead on a headland north of Stiklestad, nothing remarkable except no one has lived here for twenty years, salt air, pine smoke, a man with a water-drip and a measurement | The witnessed truth that cannot be officially named, the accidental precision of a measurement made in the middle of a battle |
| Tuonela Boundary Shore | TUO | The edge of the Finnish underworld — a black river, reed-banks, cold that is different from weather-cold, the far shore visible and unreachable for the living | The boundary between living and dead, methodical love, the instrument of recovery |
| Nidaros — Olaf's Shrine City | NID | Norwegian harbor city, salt air, timber and stone, a cathedral being built over a saint's tomb, the Danish governor's compound at the harbor, pilgrims already arriving | Martyrdom as authority, the argument death makes that life could not, the institutional hinge of canonization |
| Völva's Heath | VLH | A flat northern heath with the grass pressed flat in a circle, pre-dawn, the standing stone at the field's edge in the distance, the quality of air after something large has been said in the invisible register | Prophetic transaction, truth without utility, the record-keeping that follows doom |
| Alrekstaðir Temple | ALR | A round stone temple on a low hill above the fjord, the sacred fire burning in an iron bowl at the altar's center, priests who have tended this flame without interruption for three generations, the smell of pine resin and cold sea in every room | Continuous sacred transmission, the original source, the thing that cannot be replicated only carried |
| Balder's Grove, Sognefjord | BLG | A newly-rebuilt timber temple at the fjord's edge, the wood still pale and unseasoned, Frithiof on his knees at the cold altar, the fjord visible through the open door, the smell of fresh-cut pine waiting for fire | Reparation, the completion of penance, the moment when reconstruction becomes consecration |
| Starkad's Farmstead | STK | A desolate holding at the edge of the settled Danish coast: one building, one fire, one enormous ruined chair; the smell of old iron and ash; the sea audible but not visible; the world contracted to what one very old man still needs | The divided life at its end, the compelled act and its carrier, the weight that cannot be put down and cannot be balanced |
| Danish Coastal Road and Harbor Settlement | DAN | Flat marshland coast running from the outer settlements toward Lejre: a gravel road through reed-beds, a small harbor village at the midpoint, torchlight at the harbormaster's post, the road continuing inland toward the royal district | The contested threshold between private testimony and official record, the road where institutional interests intercept personal truth |
| Laugar — Guðrún's Farmstead | LGR | Hot-springs farmstead in Dalir, western Iceland: steam rising from the ground year-round, stone outbuildings, a great longhouse with the door facing the road, the constant smell of sulfur under wool-smoke, the hot pools visible in any weather | Pride as architecture, jealousy's domestic space, the kept object as standing accusation, the mind that will not release |
| Sælingsdals Ford | LXF | A cold, shallow river crossing between Laugar territory and Herðholt territory: flat stones underwater, fast current at the center, one bent rowan on the near bank, the exact width of a spear-throw; the boundary between two families who should never have needed a boundary | Contested passage, the geography of feud, the threshold between rival claims, the point where it becomes too late to turn around |
| Herðholt — Ólafr the Peacock's Hall | HHL | A great Norse farmstead at the head of the valley: carved beams depicting the stories of the gods, a wide fire-hearth at the center, the carved chest in the corner, a long view down through the gate to the Laxá river below; the finest hall in Iceland, now also a monument | Grief held in grandeur, the father who outlived the son, the archive of what was taken, the closed chest as final form |
| Roncevaux Pass | RON | A high mountain pass in the Pyrenees: cold grey rock, wind that does not stop, the smell of old blood in the shale; twenty thousand burial mounds visible from the road; the place where the sound of a horn was heard thirty miles away and was not heard soon enough | Pride and its cost, the decision made too late, the grief that does not know what to do with itself, the memorial that has not yet been built |
| Pyrenean High Road | PYR | The mountain road north from Roncevaux: exposed ridgeline, cold even in summer, a pass above treeline where anyone on the road can see anyone else from a long distance; the road that survivors took, and that pursuers know to watch | The passage between grief and duty, contested passage in high open ground, the road that must be traveled to bring something back |
| Aix-la-Chapelle — Charlemagne's Chapel | AIX | Charlemagne's palatine chapel at his capital: octagonal stone, high ceiling, the reliquary space adjacent to the altar, the smell of incense and old wax; the institutional memory of the Frankish empire in architectural form | Memorial-making, the place where what happened is fixed in physical form for everyone who comes after, the choice of which version of the truth is permanent |
| London — Buckingham's Residence | LON | A grand Stuart-era English townhouse: high ceilings, portrait-hung walls, candlewax and wool, a private study where state business and private feeling share the same desk; beautiful and already ending | Love expressed through objects entrusted to others, the personal cost of political decisions, the graceful giving-back of what cannot be kept |
| Calais Harbor | CDV | A Channel port in early-modern France: salt and rope and crowded quays, harbor officers with administrative authority, the smell of tide and ship-tar, the English coast visible on clear days and invisible on all the days that matter | The controlled threshold, institutional delay as weapon, the point where bureaucratic obstruction and physical urgency meet directly |
| Spychów — Jurand's Castle | JUR | A timber-and-stone fortified manor on the Polish-Teutonic border in Mazovia: frost-hardened earth, a small village under the castle's shadow, pine smoke and horse smell; a frontier holding that has been fighting the same war for twenty years | The household diminished by institutional violence, the fighter's grief made precise, the frontier between law and what the law cannot see |
| Teutonic Border Crossing | TKT | A fortified timber gate on the Order's territorial boundary: raised road through flat marshland, two watchtowers, a customs post, the black-cross banner over the gate; cold, procedurally exact, staffed by men trained to treat every crossing as potentially hostile | Institutional threshold, the gate that decides what the law on the other side gets to know, the point where evidence must be concealed or legitimized |
| Kraków — Polish Royal Court | KRK | The royal capital of Poland: stone streets, a castle above the city, the chancery building adjacent to the cathedral, clerks and diplomats in the anteroom, the smell of beeswax and ink and diplomatic tension; the place where competing versions of events are entered into the permanent record | Competing testimony, institutional record-making, the court that decides what the official past looks like |
| Livery Stable District | STB | A walled-city stable district at the German imperial city's periphery: horse-smell and mud, iron fittings and cheap tallow, the skull of an old horse nailed above the main arch in the traditional protective way; the specific silence of someone tending geese who has been speaking their true name to the dead every morning for forty days | The impossible witness, the displaced rightful person, the truth spoken where no suppression was expected |
| Trade Guild Court | GLD | The guild arbiters' hall in the commercial district: two senior arbiters at a long table, beeswax lamps, the smell of old paper and registry ink, the lectern where competing claims are presented; the room where institutional records and the actual past are forced to negotiate with each other | Competing registrations, the prior consistent statement, the moment where evidence is received or refused |
| Faithful John's Restoration Room | DNG | A stone room adjacent to the dungeon in a German prince's castle: a writing table, a narrow window, cold lamp-light, the smell of fresh ink and old stone; not technically a cell — the servant restored from stone lives here because he cannot appear publicly without triggering the execution order still technically open; the folio sealed and waiting on the table | The silence that was itself the service, the explanation that can now be given, the restoration that does not legally undo the sentence |
| Castle Household Corridor | CHC | The main administrative corridor of a German castle household: a standing desk, a log-book, the head steward stationed at the junction between the private rooms and the public hall; the smell of floor-wax and lampblack; the place where every message in or out receives an origin entry — the bureaucracy of the household made manifest in ink | The log that needs an origin, the named carrier as institutional attestation, the intersection of personal correspondence and household record |
| Prince's Antechamber | ANT | The waiting room before a German prince's great hall: one door out to the hall, one door back to the corridor, the chamberlain stationed between; a single bench, two candle stands, the specific silence of a room whose function is to pause whatever arrives; the chamberlain's editorial mandate over what reaches the prince emotionally unprepared | The right of preview, delivery-to-hand as distinct from delivery-to-chamberlain, the last institutional gate before the personal |
| Prince's Great Hall | PRH | The main audience chamber of a German prince's castle: high-timbered ceiling, a fire at the far end, two household men near the wall, the prince at the high seat with an open book he is not reading; the afternoon quality of light through narrow windows; the room where formal and informal life are not separated — the prince here is alone with himself and also in the position that requires a response | The account read slowly, the silence after eight pages, the seven words the prince was going to say |
| Gretchen's Prison Tower | MGR | A high-walled tower in a German imperial city: thick stone, narrow grate window, straw and tallow, the chaplain's hourly visits the only human contact, the executioner's footsteps audible two floors below; the cell of a woman who has already decided how her story ends | Condemnation, the right to be heard, the grief that cannot be rescued, self-determination within judgment |
| Clerk's Deathbed House | CLK | A small residential house near the civic quarter of a German imperial city: a sickroom on the ground floor, the notary already gone, a dying man holding a sealed recantation that names a magistrate still in office; old paper and tallow and the smell of a city that has forgotten the man it condemned; the document finished and waiting for a carrier | The obligation to correct the record even when correction cannot repair what the record destroyed; the clerk who lived with the lie and did not survive the truth |
| Dominican Legal Court | DKN | The Dominican friars' legal court adjacent to the civic square: white-washed walls, a lectern-desk, clerks in black habits, the smell of lime and old tallow; the permanent tension between ecclesiastical jurisdiction and civic law; the desk where a family's wealth is more present than a prisoner's rights | Competing jurisdictions, documents as weapons, institutional pressure on the record-keeper, the moment where the writ and the statute argue |
| Lisuarte's Tournament Precinct | LTN | A coastal English tournament field in the aftermath of single combat: pennants still flying, the grass torn deep from two heavy men fighting for two hours, a dark stain where Ardan Canileo fell, the notary's canvas booth at the field's edge, the herald's vestibule lodge at the far end; the smell of horse and churned earth and lamp-oil; the specific silence of a place where something decisive has already happened and the paperwork has not yet caught up | The aftermath of triumph, the unwritten margin, the record still open, the service that precedes victory and is not included in its account |
| Herald's Lodge Vestibule | CHT | The low-ceilinged antechamber of a master herald's lodge: four clerks at long tables copying submissions into the day's register, a standing desk with a submission log, the inner corridor beyond a heavy door with two men in a great lord's livery positioned in it; the smell of tallow and fresh ink and old leather bindings; the room where what was done in the field becomes what was done in the record | Institutional gatekeeping, the engineered delay, the corridor between evidence and permanent record, the second battle that combat's aftermath always requires |
| Peña Pobre — Poor Rock Hermitage | PBK | A small grey island off the coastal headland, reached by sea: salt-grass and stone, a chapel at the high point, a winter garden tended for forty years by the same hands; the hermit Andalod at his table; the site of six months of dissolution and recovery, of the name Beltenebros spoken and remembered, of the right name spoken when the exile ended; the island that can only be reached by someone who understands what happened there | The anonymous exile, the hermit's witness to both names, the document that can only be completed here, the seal date in three days |
| Queen Philippa's Pavilion | PHI | A campaign tent in the English siege camp outside Calais, 1347: canvas walls dyed Plantagenet blue and gold, too many candles burning in the morning, a physician's smell and a writing desk with the sealing wax still warm; the queen seven months pregnant and entirely composed; the weight of eleven months' siege audible outside; the specific quiet of a room where a decision has already been made and the only question is whether the mechanism arrives in time | The architecture of mercy, the petition as instrument, the queen who designs her intercession so the king can say yes without breaking |
| Campaign Adjutant's Registry | CPT | A forward command tent in the English siege camp: board floor, three long clerk tables, the main desk where formal orders are drafted and stamped; the campaign log open beside the adjutant's seal; the execution order in fair-copy progress on the desk; outside, the sound of the English army waiting for noon; the distance between what the order says and what happens measured in four lines of a clerk's careful hand | The irrevocability of sealed orders, the campaign log as prior record, the thirty-year paperwork mind that understands exactly what it is enabling |
| French Royal Antechamber | FRK | The antechamber and privy corridor of the French royal residence in Paris, 1364: high stone corridor, cold in spring, two candle stands flanking the inner door, a usher at the privy chamber entrance with the session entry log; thirty feet of antechamber crowded with petitioners and minor nobles; the privy council session audible through the door; one chief councillor who has already stepped aside because he understood the argument and hated it | The obligation that arrives with the news, the council that loves the king by trying to protect him from himself, the word that lets a man say the hardest thing without asking for agreement |
| Sierra Morena Roadside Inn | SMR | A low stone inn on the Sierra Morena mountain road in Castile: wine-smell and old straw, a tired sergeant cataloguing confiscated property in the back room, chains and leg-irons on the table, a leather folder under the property bag; the specific after-silence of a place where a lunatic with good intentions has recently passed through and everything is still being sorted out | The aftermath of idealism, confiscated records, the tired official who just wants to do the right thing without knowing what it is |
| Camino Real Magistrate's Post | CMG | A stone building at a road fork on the Castilian plain: a transit magistrate's ledger on the desk, the road going two directions out the front, a parish priest with diocesan authority on the road further on; flat dry Castilian landscape that has looked exactly this way since before the Romans; the administrative weight of a place that logs everything and the narrow road where jurisdictions meet and one of them has bailiffs | The ambiguity of confiscated personal property, the clerical review right, the road where two kinds of authority disagree about which one applies |
| Montiel Fair Ground | MNT | A traveling fair on the Castilian plain near Montiel: canvas booths, animal smell, a puppet theater at the edge where a man with a monkey on his shoulder is half-packed and watching the road approach; the kind of ground that is always becoming somewhere else; the specific way a man who calls himself Maese Pedro is precisely as real as he needs to be | The reinvented self, the puppet theater as the life story continued in another form, the moment a man receives back the record of who he was before he became who he is |
| Magistrate's Evidence Room La Mancha | MAS | A small town magistrate's office in La Mancha: the evidence room with the contested brass object in a cloth bag, carrying document on the cord, both descriptions sealed; the magistrate who has had the barber's son and the knight's companions in his office for two weeks and still cannot determine whether the thing is a basin or a helmet; dry Castilian light, a shelf of unresolved property disputes, two witnesses waiting in the hall | The object that is simultaneously two things, institutional inability to resolve a genuine ontological dispute, the archive as the site where undecidable questions wait |
| Crown Notary's Office Alcalá de Henares | ACL | The crown notary's office in Alcalá de Henares: long tables, the official classification ledger, the notary who must file what cannot be resolved into one category; the clerk's writing station, the section of the ledger reserved for contested property, the stamp that means not resolved but preserved; the smell of official wax and the afternoon light on a city that exists precisely because it is the place where records are kept | The archivist who preserves both truths by filing the question rather than answering it, the administrative act that honors contradiction, the document that outlasts the dispute |

| Copyist's Quarter | CVP | A cramped commercial district of the old port city: book-stalls and ink-smell, three-storey buildings with workshops on the ground floor, parchment and vellum hanging to dry in the narrow alleys, the smell of shellfish dye from the neighboring tanners; a specific kind of quiet where commerce and scholarship share the same staircase | Manuscript provenance, authentication, the trade in intellectual property, objects that carry biographical evidence |
| Melinda Scholar's Court | MLN | A scribal house off the East African trading port of Melinda: straw matting on the stone floor, high windows admitting salt air, shelves of coastal geographical records and traditional accounts in Arabic and Swahili; Ahmed ibn Yusuf's study at the far end where the light is best; the harbor visible through the window, the Portuguese factor's desk visible in the harbor building below; the specific silence of a room where careful work has been completed and the problem of getting it out is now the only remaining question | Prior knowledge, the indigenous record that predates the arrival, the scholar who has been observing the observers |
| Lisbon Humanist Archive | LHA | A stone building in the Lisbon scholarly district — not the maritime court, not the royal commission, but the humanist foundation's archive: long intake tables, a classification ledger that accepts cultural records from any tradition, Frei Tomás's desk with his provenance requirements; the specific quality of an institution that has decided its mandate is broader than the state's convenience; the intake log open, the stylus ready, the column where the compiler's name goes | The record that precedes the claim, the archive that does not require the knowledge to be useful to the empire in order to preserve it, the intake log as the instrument of inclusion |
| Mountain Monastery Road | MDN | A Benedictine monastery on a limestone hill north of the city: whitewashed stone walls, a scriptorium building separate from the chapel, an ancient cloister garden with a single bitter-orange tree, Father Bento's cell with its view down the hill to the sea; the smell of old binding leather and damp stone; the road below visible from the upper cloister walk, showing anyone who approaches in time | Institutional procedure versus conscience, the keeper of prior knowledge, the rule that should not apply here |
| Scribe's Port City | SIT | A working port city with a commercial scribes' district: hired writers' offices above the chandlers' row, the smell of salt and ink, storage chests of completed work under beds; a professional man's room with a view of the harbor and a chest he wants emptied | The professional witness, the account that sits in storage too long, the transfer of a record that has been kept in the wrong kind of attention |
| Port Registry Building | PRT | A harbor-front administrative building where documents about extraordinary events are logged before transfer to permanent archives: a long intake desk, a transit registrar with a classification manual, the loading dock visible through the rear window; the specific quiet of a place that has received every kind of claim and is not impressed | Receiving extraordinary evidence in ordinary form, the intake that determines what category a thing belongs to, the transit point between experience and record |
| Refugee Camp Outside Troy | IDC | A sprawling camp in the olive groves beyond the blackened walls: lean-tos and salvage shelters, the smell of ash carried on every wind, the sea visible through the ruins of the east gate, old men sitting in the specific quiet of people who have outlived the thing they organized their lives around; Idaeus's lean-to at the camp's edge, where one very old herald sits with a clay tablet in his pack and no audience | The witness who outlived the event, the document without a venue, the last carrier of a record everyone else has stopped needing |
| Messene — Guest-Gift Witness's House | MSN | A hillside house in Messene: an elderly woman's room with a table and two chairs, the notary's draft attestation on the table, a view of the Messenian plain through the small window; Dexithea's careful hands and the stylus she is holding and the story her grandfather told her about a friendship that never got to share a table; the specific quiet of a place where a memory is about to become a document | Guest-friendship property, the xenia obligation that survives the gift's use, the woman who carries the story of a transaction that predates her by three generations, the document that establishes prior category before competing claims can be evaluated |
| Ithaca Estate Registry | ITH | The registry office near the Ithaca harbor: Polyphron the registrar at his desk, the suitors' preliminary filing in the pending column, the classification ledger open to the disputed property section; a harbor smell through the window and the sound of ships unloading; the room where the prior-category rule determines whether the attestation controls or merely competes | The bow's legal category, guest-friendship property before household property, the registry that establishes what kind of thing an object is before adjudicating who owns it |
| Scheria — Alcinous's Palace and Harbor | PHC | The Phaeacian island's palace complex and its working harbor: long stone palace corridors with light from the sea, the archive room off the women's quarter, the harbor gate where the steward stands; the palace still in operation after Odysseus sailed; the archive room where sealed private documents age on shelves waiting for a determination that never came; the harbor road to the outside world | The unsent letter, the private document of a private person bearing a royal seal, the question of what ownership means for something that was never transmitted, the three-year administrative delay |
